
## report_84 note (Nov 14 2026): vector state as a bias/confirmation layer
Tested vector state (BULL_NEW <=10d, BULL_MID, BULL_OLD, BEAR) as context for four generic entries (pullback, 20d breakout, volume thrust, 5d drop) plus a state-only baseline, 10-day open-to-open excess return vs equal-weight universe, net 30bp. Pass bar (both eras >=0.5pp, pooled clustered t>=2 on >=3 of 4 signals) failed for NEW-BEAR and ANYBULL-BEAR. State alone is ~0 (-0.01pp pooled). Fresh-bull THRUST is worse (-0.77pp, t -2.0). Only weak hint: DROP in bull state +0.35pp pooled (t +1.4), not a pass. Conclusion: bull state is not a general bullish bias for other entries here; the vector's value is the rare start-of-move flip, not a persistent tilt. Survivorship-biased daily universe; 1h not tested.

## report_85 note (Nov 14 2026): 13F fund buys x vector flip
15 declared funds (11 concentrated, 4 quant incl. Renaissance), flag live from filing date for 90d, 1,893 CUSIPs mapped to universe tickers. Flagged vs unflagged flips: ANY pooled -0.7pp (t -0.3), QUANT +0.5pp / -1.2pp by era, CONC +7.0pp / -3.2pp (t -2.9 in 2022+; n only 410 per era; 2019-21 gain is lumpy). 60d excess after a 13F buy is public: negative for CONC (-2.5% / -3.8%) and ~-0.3% for QUANT. No pass. Quant-fund flags are non-selective (57% of flips flagged); 45-day filing lag means the buying is already old. Not tested: concentrated-fund aggregate count of >=3 funds (too few events), 13F position-size changes of top-decile conviction.

## report_86 note (Nov 14 2026): what makes a flip big (context + supply + payoff)
Declared H1 (compressed pre-flip range better) and H2 (extended / post-blow-off flips worse) both rejected: direction is the OPPOSITE. Flips after range expansion (ATR10/ATR60 high) beat compressed ones, +1.2pp (2019-21) / +1.3pp (2022+), pooled t +2.1, small and post-hoc, medians worse; extension off the 60d low also tilts toward more huge moves (2.6% vs 0.6% huge, pooled), not significant. H3 (supply) is the strongest structure seen: huge-flip rate and mean fall monotonically with price and dollar volume in both eras (<$5 huge 3.5-16% vs 0.6-1.1% for $20+; <$1M dollar volume huge 3.6-6.7% vs 0.8-1.7% for $25M+). Caveats: tiny n for <$2 (81 / 213), survivorship bias is worst there, 30bp costs are unrealistic below ~$1M dollar volume. Payoff bootstrap: 2019-21 needs ~100+ flips/year for P(year>0) 88%, ~250 for 98%; 2022+ whole-universe mean is ~0 so P(year>0) stays ~50% at any K. Median year without its single best trade is negative below K=50. Move size looks supply/liquidity driven, not institution-driven.

## report_87 note (Nov 14 2026): winners vs failures inside small/low-priced flips ($1-10, n 5,085)
Base rates: huge 5.8% (2019-21) / 2.6% (2022+), win10 21% / 16%. No predeclared feature passed. Flip-bar volume ratio is the only consistent one: top tercile +16.5pp / +3.4pp over bottom, pooled +7.2pp (t +1.9), huge rate 7.8% vs 5.4% and 4.0% vs 1.2%, AUC(win10) 0.55-0.56; same direction both eras, just misses the t>=2 bar. Prior 20d return tilts the WRONG way (already-moving names win more, AUC 0.55). Deeper-below-year-high, longer coil and higher dollar volume (lower is better here) all fail or reverse. Reading: inside small caps, winners look like names already in motion on heavy volume (attention), not coiled/neglected ones. Winner-vs-failure remains mostly unpredictable from price/volume; next lever is attention data (news/social/options) rather than more OHLCV.

## report_88 note (Nov 14 2026): penny/low-priced intraday (60 days of 5m, ~995 names, ONE regime, leads only)
Part 1 timeframe grid (vector long flips on 5m/15m/30m/1h RTH bars, 12/8/8/7-bar horizons): gross excess vs control ~0 (5m -0.01pp, 15m -0.01, 30m +0.24 t 1.2, 1h -0.04); every timeframe negative at 50bp+ costs. No intraday-frame edge on pennies in this window. 1m not testable (7 days of history). Part 2 day-of-flip: trigger T = prev level + 0.35*prev ATR reproduces 435/437 daily flips. Entering when T is crossed intraday is ~3.5-4.1% cheaper than the confirmed-close next-open entry on the same true flips (paired +2.3 to +3.3pp, 90% still green at the flip-day close) -- Rey's "we enter so late" is real. But 547 false crosses (closed back under T) vs 437 true flips; false-cross leg -9.9% mean winsorized (median -2.3%, 16% worse than -20%). Trading every cross: -6.5% vs system -5.6% gross. The entry gain is eaten by unconfirmable crosses unless something separates true from false crosses at the time of the cross (volume pace by time of day, price vs T by minutes, gap size). Window is a falling penny tape (control negative), n small. Next if pursued: classify true vs false crosses with information available at the cross; needs more than 60 days.

## report_89 note (Nov 14 2026): gap tax, true-vs-false cross classifier, flip contagion
(A) The close-to-next-open gap on daily flips is tiny (+0.20% mean 2019-21, +0.02% 2022+; $1-10 +0.41% / +0.11%); entering at the flip-bar close instead of next open adds +0.32pp (t 2.1) in 2019-21 and nothing in 2022+. So lateness is the confirmation lag, not the overnight gap. Late-day detection is feasible: price at 15:55 > T catches 92% of flip days with 79% precision (60d 5m); the false-alarm return figure printed (-66%) is outlier/data-mismatch contaminated and not trusted. (B) No classifier separates true from false intraday crosses: VOLPACE AUC 0.57 / 0.51 by half, TMIN, GAP, EXT fail; high-VOLPACE crosses are less bad (-0.6% vs -7.4% every-cross mean) but not stable. (C) Flip contagion is opposite to the declared hypothesis: more same-industry flips in the prior 5 days is WORSE (top-bottom tercile -4.0pp 2019-21, -1.6pp 2022+, pooled -2.5pp t -2.2; 2022+ t -2.7). Post-hoc direction, same sign both eras: lead. Reading: isolated/idiosyncratic flips beat flips inside an already-flipping theme (the theme move is late by the time peers have flipped).

## report_90 note (Nov 14 2026): stacked flip score, strict split (train 2019-21, frozen test 2022-26Q1)
Features: VOLX, PEERS, VR, EXT, R60, BLOW, log dollar volume, log price. Spec 1 (rank-sum, signs from train Spearman IC vs return) FAILED by design flaw: rank IC follows the median, which runs opposite to the tail/mean for volume and extension features, so the score came out inverted (top-bottom -3.0pp t -2.7 on test). Not re-signed after seeing test. Spec 2 (ridge logistic on rank features, target trade return >= +10%, trained 2019-21 only, frozen) passes the declared bar on test: ALL top-minus-bottom quintile +2.8pp (t +2.1, AUC 0.563), $1-10 +9.2pp (t +2.8, AUC 0.582), $10+ +0.5pp (t +0.5, no). Coefs (rank features): EXT +0.50, VOLX +0.22, VR +0.21, PEERS -0.22, LPX -0.14, LDV -0.13. Robustness: the top quintile is a high-variance bucket (median -8.1%, 40% of trades lose >10% vs 6% in the bottom quintile), so the gain is tail-driven. $1-10 top-minus-bottom is positive every test year (2022 +2.2, 2023 +6.2, 2024 +5.3, 2025 +11.4, 2026 +12.3pp); ex-top-3 top quintile +3.6% vs -2.4% bottom; ex-top-1% +1.5% vs -1.7%. ALL-universe edge is carried by 2025 (+9.3pp) and flat elsewhere: fragile. Lead: stacked score on $1-10 small caps; AUC ~0.58 stays far below the 0.65+ the dream scenario needs. Survivorship-flattered; costs for small caps understated.

## report_91 note (Nov 14 2026): find the mover first, use the vector for entry/stop ($1-10, +15% day on >=3x volume, n 1,180)
Buying the mover next open (10d hold, net 30bp): +6.7pp / +2.5pp mean 10d EXCESS in 2019-21 / 2022+ for vector-BULL movers (median +2.5% / -1.1%, ex-top3 +3.5% / +1.5%); BEAR movers +1.4 / +2.6pp. H1 vector state adds nothing (BULL-BEAR +5.2pp t 1.3 then -0.1pp t -0.4; pooled +1.4pp t 0.3): FAIL. H2 hold-to-vector-exit raises the mean (+85.9% 2019-21 bubble-driven, +13.4% 2022+) but medians are -0.8% / -11.4% and 53% of 2022+ trades lose >10%: lottery shape, not a risk-managed trade. Vector level is not a usable stop on a mover day: median initial risk 26-30% of entry. H3 pullback to the vector level (risk ~6%) is worse: 2022+ mean +8.8% but median -8.4%, 37% >10% losers, return/risk median -1.6. H4 waiting for the bear mover's bull flip (46% flip within 20d in 2022+) is worse than buying now (-1.0% vs +2.5%). Reading: in this workflow the edge is the mover selection (continuation after a volume spike, small but both eras), not the vector; the vector's value is as a trailing exit on the tail. Survivorship-flattered, 30bp costs understated, 2019-21 flattered by the 2020-21 bubble.

## report_92 note (Nov 15 2026): short side / stop-and-reverse
Shorting every daily bear flip (cover at open after bull flip, avg hold 38-41d) loses in every bucket and era, even with zero borrow cost: ALL -2.0% (2019-21) / -2.4% (2022+), $1-10 -4.1% / -4.1%; at 15% annualized borrow -4.2% to -6.6%; win rate 17-24%; median -5% to -8%. Tail: 0.2-0.7% of shorts lose >=50% (worst -253% to -378%, includes a few data-glitch-looking prints; ex-worst-3 does not change the mean). Stop-and-reverse (long bull + short bear) per trade at 15% borrow: ALL 2019-21 +0.26%, 2022+ -2.34%; $1-10 +6.2% / -1.9%: the short side is a drag. 1H pennies (60d, falling tape): shorts -0.3% at zero borrow (-0.8% at 15%), better than longs (-4.0%) but still negative. Reading: the bear flip confirms AFTER the drop; small caps bounce from the bearish phase (mean reversion in the bear state), so bear = 'not long', not 'short'. Survivorship (missing delisted names) biases shorts DOWN, so true short results are better than shown, but borrow availability/fees and squeezes are not in the data. GEVO's pre-flip decline (1.4 to 1.08, ~-20%) is a hindsight example; the aggregate says otherwise.

## report_93/94 note (Nov 15 2026): which bear flips are worth shorting
Base short (daily bear flip, 30bp + 15% borrow) -4.6% mean per trade on 21-31k flips. No selector makes it positive. Report 93: blow-off tops (high 60d extension, large preceding bull leg) no better (+0.2pp, t -0.2 / +0.3pp t 1.5); names already in a downtrend (low 60d return) are WORSE shorts (-5.1% vs -4.3% for already-bounced names, pooled t -3.6; the bear flip arrives after the drop and these bounce); low-volatility/chop names are the LEAST bad shorts (ATR% bottom third -3.6% vs top third -5.7%, pooled t +5.4, opposite to the declared hypothesis, post-hoc) but still negative in both eras. Report 94: flip-bar volume >= 1.5x does not help (flagged -4.9% vs -4.5%), the vector+EMA stack (21<55, close<55) is slightly WORSE (-5.1% vs -4.3%), volume+EMA worse still (-5.5%); insider sales (Form 4 S by officers/directors >= $50k in prior 30d, available 2025-02..2026-03 only, n 1,088) -5.4% vs -6.3% unflagged, not a selector. Conclusion: the bear state is 'not long', not a short signal; confluence of the vector's own components does not rescue it. The JMIA/ABEV/AEHL shorts seen on charts are hindsight on a selection (those names are on the loser list because they had just turned bear), and survivorship (missing delisted names) plus unmodeled borrow/squeezes leave the true short result unmeasured.

## Addendum 6 (Nov 15 2026): where the theory stands after reports 84-95
Frame: the vector is a rule-based pivot-ratchet state machine (no learned parameters; its Pine title says "SVM - Fractals" but the code is not a support vector machine) that classifies BULL/BEAR state with a confirmation lag. It labels state; it does not size the move. A flip pays only when the forward move beats the lag tax (~15% late entry, ~13% give-back on the big legs). Returns come from a rare tail (top 3 winners = 60-70% of some books; median flip loses ~5%), so the question is selecting moves larger than the lag.
Why it 'dies': (1) the 2019-21 universe mean was +4.6% per flip in a speculative boom with mean-reverting daily VR 0.7-0.9; 2022+ the whole-universe mean is ~0 (+0.2%), so with no tail selection there is nothing left; (2) the tail lives in low-priced / low-dollar-volume names (huge rate 3.5-16% under $5 vs 0.6-1.1% above $20), i.e. supply/attention, not institutions; (3) survivorship flatters the tail names; (4) confirmation lag is the price, not a bug (gap-to-next-open is ~0.0-0.4%).
What participant data said: 13F fund buys (15 funds incl. Renaissance) no pass; insider and catalyst proxies lumpy; volume at the flip bar is the one durable tilt; attention (movers) shows small continuation (+2.5 to +6.7pp/10d) but the vector adds nothing to it.
Leads (not yet traded): (a) stacked ridge-logistic flip score, frozen from 2019-21, AUC 0.563 all / 0.582 on $1-10, top-minus-bottom quintile +2.8pp / +9.2pp, positive every test year on $1-10; (b) isolated flips (few same-industry peers flipping first) beat crowded-theme flips (-2.5pp pooled t -2.2); (c) 1H flips catch moves earlier than daily (GEVO: 1H flip Oct 27 ~+57% marked vs daily flip ~flat) but 96% of 1H penny flips lose small; (d) 2y yield 60d change as the one fast regime driver; (e) squeeze regime filter (live). All survivorship-flattered, small-cap costs understated.
Dead ends (do not repeat): 13F/index/13D/biotech catalysts as gates, EMA/trend gates, open-entry on gap, hard stops, fade exits, bottom-cutting models, OHLCV move-size classifier (AUC 0.52-0.58), vector state as bias for other entries (report_84), vector level as a stop on movers (26-30% away), pullback-to-level entries, shorting bear flips or selected bear flips (report_92-94), ML bake-off (report_95): RBF SVM test AUC 0.484, gradient boosting 0.517 (train 0.72), random forest 0.553, logistic 0.563 best; nonlinear models overfit this feature set, so more model complexity is not the missing piece.
Missing: survivorship-free point-in-time prices (biggest), realistic small-cap costs/borrow, real-time participant data (options flow via Theta, shadow-score ledger on the Mac), more than 60 days of intraday history for 1H/5m penny work.

## report_96/97 note (Nov 15 2026): state-machine path analysis and duration classifier
Report 96 (850k in-position state-days, 31k flips): distance of close above the ratchet (DIST, ATRs) strongly predicts the 10-day bear-flip hazard (44-48% when <1.5 ATR, 16-19% at 1.5-3, 6-7% at 3-5, 2-3% at 5+) but barely predicts forward return (fwd10 +0.9% to +0.6% in 2019-21, +0.3% to -0.1% in 2022+; stretched states a hair lower in both eras, 0.3-0.4pp): the state machine says how long the state is likely to last, not how much is left, so state-conditioned exits (cut near the level, take profit when stretched) have no return edge and hold-to-bear is consistent with the data. Ratchet-raise count NR is almost always 0 on daily bars (uninformative here). Frozen score quintiles (test 2022+): Q4 flips stay alive longer (57% alive at day 20 vs 42% in Q0), stretch further (median max-DIST 2.7 vs 2.0), carry 36% of the positive return (equal share 20%), yet have a WORSE median final return (-7.8% vs -3.4%; Spearman(score, final return) -0.215): lottery shape. Report 97: the same eight rank features predict flip DURATION a bit better than flip return: ridge logistic TEST AUC alive-at-day-20 0.584 (alive60 0.568) vs 0.563 for win10; coefficients put the weight on flip-bar volume (+0.44), range expansion VR (+0.31) and volatility blow-off (+0.30). Top-vs-bottom quintile by duration score: alive20 62% vs 42%, trade mean +1.7% vs -0.5% (spread +2.2pp t +2.4, huge 2.1% vs 0.7%). Misses the declared 0.60 AUC bar (no pass on that criterion, spread criterion passes). Reading: the model is a persistence classifier (volume + volatility expansion at the flip -> the state lasts), which matches the persistence framework; the tail comes from duration, not from direction.

## report_98 note (Nov 15 2026): survival model with early confirmation
Flips alive at the close of day 3 (n 25,930; test 16,073). Adding day-3 state features (D3 = distance above the ratchet in ATRs, RET3, volume persistence, up-closes) lifts TEST AUC for 'still bullish at day 20' from 0.576 (base features only) to 0.733 (H1 passes); D3 alone is 0.73 (RET3 0.66): the state machine's own cushion above its level predicts its own survival, consistent with report_96's hazard table. But H2 FAILS: entering at the day-4 open with the base+EARLY top quintile (85% alive at day 20) earns +0.4% mean (median -7.1%, huge 1.3%) vs +2.8% for the base-score top quintile entering day 1; for all survivors day-4 entry +0.3% vs day-1 +1.3% (median day1-to-day4 open move +0.5%, mean +1.0%). Persistence is predictable once the move has started but the return was in the first bars: high-D3 flips are already extended. Duration prediction and return capture are different problems; the lag tax is paid in the first 1-3 bars and cannot be bought back by waiting for confirmation. Use of D3-like state: a risk/hold signal (how likely the state is to survive), not an entry filter.

## report_99 note (Nov 15 2026): physics-style pressure / supply / self-excitation measures (point-in-time shares from SEC cover pages, 25,671 of 30,323 flips covered)
Excursion size (PEAK = max high during the hold / entry - 1) is the predictable quantity: month-clustered Spearman IC with PEAK is +0.26 for P1 = ATR/close*sqrt(flip-bar volume ratio) (t 14 / 21, both eras) but ATRP alone is +0.28 / +0.26, so H1 FAILS: square-root pressure adds nothing over plain volatility. Supply (H2) PASSES robustly: lower market cap and higher true turnover (flip-bar volume / shares outstanding) predict larger excursions (MCAP IC -0.139 / -0.124, TURN +0.167 / +0.166, all |t| > 7) and higher huge-rate in both eras: smallest-cap tercile huge 3.8% (2019-21) / 1.9% (2022+) vs largest 0.8% / 0.5%; top-turnover tercile 3.4% / 1.9% vs bottom 1.1% / 0.5%; mean trade return smallest-cap +10.0% / +1.4% vs largest +1.1% / -0.7%. Self-excitation proxies (H3) fail: prior-20d volume-burst count vs duration IC +0.016 (t 1.3) then +0.048 (t 4.6), volume lag-1 autocorrelation ~0. Rank IC with the trade RETURN is negative for P1, ATRP, TURN (-0.17 to -0.31): more volatile / higher-turnover flips have bigger excursions but worse median realized returns (give-back, lag). Incremental value: adding the physics set to the report_90 features lifts TEST AUC for win10 only 0.567 to 0.576 and leaves alive-at-20d at 0.588 to 0.584. Reading: supply (market cap, turnover) is the real, stable determinant of how far a flip can run; pressure physics and Hawkes-type clustering proxies add nothing beyond volatility and volume; none of it converts to selectable return without selecting the tail itself.

## report_100 note (Nov 15 2026): portfolio returns and max drawdown, model vs unfiltered book
Max 20 positions, 5% of STARTING capital each (additive), entry next open, exit open after the bear flip, 30bp (small caps 100bp sensitivity), TEST 2022-01..2026-02. Random-order books averaged over 20 tie-break draws (slot capacity makes single draws very noisy: unfiltered book total ranges -27%..+44%). Unfiltered flips: +3.7% total (+0.9%/yr), maxDD -52pts, Sharpe 0.03; with flip-bar volume >=1.5x: +25.7% (-20..+74), maxDD -46, Sharpe 0.21; frozen stacked score top 20% (cutoff from the train distribution): +90.0% (+21.8%/yr), maxDD -52, Sharpe 0.54, calmar 0.42, by year -8.9/+41.2/+11.7/+20.0/+26.0 (2022..2026); $1-10 only +79% (+15..+178), maxDD -62, Sharpe 0.44; $1-10 & score +74%, maxDD -68. The score book lies outside the unfiltered book's draw range; it is NOT clearly better than the plain $1-10 filter. Reference 2019-21: unfiltered +91.5% (maxDD -69), score +47.5% (maxDD -60), $1-10 +249% (maxDD -175 as winners inflate position values): the score lowers return in the boom and helps in 2022+, so its advantage is regime-dependent. Drawdowns of 50pts on a fully invested long-only small-cap book are the cost of the tail shape; size down accordingly. Survivorship-flattered, capacity/slippage in sub-$3 names not modeled, one test window.

## report_101/102 note (Nov 15 2026): number of positions, and adding volume or insider buying to the score book (TEST 2022-01..2026-02, additive, 30bp)
Report 101: fewer slots hurt the unfiltered book badly (1/N sizing: N=3 -26%, N=5 -9%, N=10 -10%, N=20 +3.7%; random-draw ranges are huge, e.g. N=5 -79%..+108%) and the score book's Sharpe rises with N: score N=5 +0.07, N=8 +0.20, N=10 +0.37, N=15 +0.43, N=20 +0.54 (N=3 +100% on 59 trades is noise). With 5% per position the drawdown scales with exposure (score N=10 +31.8% / maxDD -28 pts, N=20 +90.0% / -52 pts) but Sharpe is the same as with 1/N sizing: breadth, not exposure, drives the risk-adjusted result because the payoff is a rare tail. Report 102: adding flip-bar volume >= 1.5x to the score HURTS (score&vol 1/N: N=5 -84%, N=10 -30%, N=20 +31.8% vs score alone +16.5% / +63.6% / +90.0%) because the score already contains volume and the high-volume subset loses the other tilts. Insider open-market purchases (officer/director >= $20k, prior 30d; only 844 flagged test flips, 189 with the score) cut drawdown but also cut return and exposure (score&ins N=20 +28.3%, maxDD -26, Sharpe 0.28, avg 8.9 positions; ins only N=20 +37.3% (27..46), maxDD -34, Sharpe 0.33; vol&ins N=20 +32.6%, maxDD -22, Sharpe 0.34, avg 10.5 positions). Nothing beats the score alone at N=20 on Sharpe (0.54). The insider books look better on drawdown per slot filled but are under-invested and thin (earlier report_77 showed this tilt is carried by its top 3 winners). Slot-limited first-come selection also wastes some of the score's ranking at small N.

## report_103/104 note (Nov 15 2026): frozen daily score on 1H flips (60 days of 5m bars Aug 20-Nov 12, ONE regime, no split possible, lead only)
3,436 1H bull flips (975 low-priced names, $1-10, funds excluded), enter next 1H open, exit after the 1H bear flip, 100bp. Mean -2.79% / median -3.65% per trade, 14% win, 5.2% >= +10%, 1.3% >= +30%; avg hold 30 bars. The frozen daily score did NOT transfer: Spearman IC vs net return -0.188, top quintile -4.18% vs bottom -2.17%, top-20 minus all -1.46pp (day-clustered t -3.4); same sign in both halves of the window and in every price bucket. Diagnostic (report_104): score predicts bigger peak excursion (median peak 1.8% in quintile 1 -> 3.9% in quintile 5; VOLX IC to peak +0.14, EXT +0.13) and slightly more >=10% winners for VOLX/PEERS, but extended names (EXT, weight .5) give it back: EXT IC to net -0.22, LPX (cheaper price) +0.18 reverses the daily sign. Mechanism guess (untested): on 1H the flip fires inside the same spike that raised EXT/VOLX, so the entry is late and the 1H exit whipsaws; on daily the lag tax is smaller relative to the move. Nothing here validates a 1H model; the 1H win must come from catching flips early (GEVO/ENGN/BGS type), not from the daily score ranking. Next test if wanted: refit a 1H-specific score needs more history than 60 days.

report_105 (Nov 15 2026): same 1H test with entry at the flip-bar 1H close and exit at the bear-flip bar 1H close (Rey's request). Result is the same as report_103: all flips mean -2.86% / median -3.71% / win 14.4% net of 100bp; score IC -0.203; top quintile -4.19% vs bottom -2.15%; top-20 minus all -1.41pp (t -3.15). Fill convention (next open vs close) moves the mean by under 0.1pp, so the negative result is not a fill artifact.

## report_106 note (Nov 15 2026): filters, a small model and exits for 1H flips (same 3,436 flips, close-to-close, 100bp; select on Aug 31-Oct 5, judge on Oct 6-Nov 12)
No filter or exit turns the 1H flips positive in this window. Passing the declared lead bar (declared sign on both halves, test day-clustered t >= 2): only risk-to-ratchet small (risk <= train median): test -1.41% vs -3.39% for the rest, good-minus-all +1.34pp t +2.53; but this is mechanical (the bear-flip exit sits at the ratchet, so loss size scales with risk; winners >= +10% barely change), it cuts losses, it does not create edge. Pre-flip 20-bar run low helps in both halves (-1.80% vs -3.08% test, t +0.85, not significant). Chop, F1 daily-bull agreement, flip-bar close location, first-hour flips: no (wrong sign or insignificant). Daily breadth: all test days fell below the train median breadth, so the split is not usable (regime shift). Ridge logistic on win>0: test IC -0.32 (it loads +0.95 on risk, which raises win share but increases loss size), so it is not a model. Exits: time stop 7 bars -1.54% (both halves ~-1.5%), 5% trailing close stop -1.78%, hard stop -4% -1.98% (test -1.42%), vs base -2.86%; all still negative. Daily-flip baseline over the same 60 days has not been run, so how much of the negative mean is this regime is unknown.

## report_107/108/109 notes (Nov 15 2026): trend-indicator pairing, chandelier exits, large caps, NQ
report_107 (daily, 30,323 flips, TRAIN 2019-21 / TEST 2022+, 30bp): pairing with SuperTrend(10,3), AlphaTrend (Kivanc, MFI 14) or Ultimate MA (PROXY: close above rising HMA21/EMA20; settings unknown) at the flip bar does NOT help: bull-minus-not is negative or insignificant on both eras (ST -1.84pp train / -0.76pp test, AT -2.49 / -0.36, UM1 -0.81 / -0.70; all three agreeing -2.09 / -0.40; month t all |t|<2); inside the frozen score top 20% the same. Indicator-bear exits (replace the vector exit) cut mean to ~0 or negative (AT test -0.26%, UM1 -0.32%, ST -0.17% vs vector +0.21%) because they cut the right tail (huge share 1.09% -> 0.03-0.35%). On the 60-day 1H flips the same agreement filters make no meaningful difference (all negative).
report_108 (chandelier = highest high - M x ATR22, close-based, vector bear backstop): on daily flips it lowers the tail and worst-1% but also the mean in train (vector +4.71%, chandelier 3x +1.04%, 5x +3.57%) and is not better in test (vector +0.21%, 3x -0.30%, 5x +0.08%); huge-share falls (1.09% -> 0.22-0.85%). Frozen score top 20% test: vector +1.94%, 5x +1.85%, 3x -0.05%. On 1H flips it halves the loss (3x -1.8% / -2.0% vs vector -3.1% / -2.6%, 2x -1.5%) but stays negative. Large caps (dv20 >= $100M, px >= $10; 572 train / 1,515 test flips): vector +4.30% / -0.47%, chandelier 4x +3.81% / +0.11%, 5x +4.30% / +0.04%, ST-bear exit +2.87% / -0.64%; no reliable edge from exits or pairing in either era.
report_109 (NQ 5m->15m/1H/4H, 2017-04..2026-07, plain vector both directions, 1 contract, 1.0 pt cost, NQ points; NOT the prop algo): vector reverse is ~zero or noise (1H train +1.7 / test +7.7 pts per trade, t +0.2 / +0.5; 4H test -45.8). Longs positive, shorts negative on every timeframe. Chandelier on 1H is the only encouraging cell: 3x +4.2 pts train / +20.3 test (t +0.8 / +1.7), 4x +6.1 / +24.0 (t +1.0 / +1.8), maxDD 2,100-2,800 pts vs 3,400-8,700 for reverse; short side improves from negative to ~0 (test +7.7 / -3.4). Below the declared bar (needs test t >= 2 and positive both eras with t) so it is a lead, not a result; 5x and 15m/4H do not hold. SuperTrend agreement on NQ lowers trade count and does not improve mean.

## report_110/111 notes (Nov 15 2026): large-cap 15m/1H chandelier; chandelier on the NQ prop algo's Rule A leg
report_110 (132 large caps, ETFs excluded, Yahoo RTH 1H 2023-12..2026-11 and 15m Aug 21-Nov 13 2026, long-only vector flips, entry at the flip-bar close, 10bp): 1H vector-only +0.69% / +0.61% per trade (train 2023-24 / test 2025+) but EXCESS over the names' own per-bar drift x bars held is +0.10% / +0.00%, i.e. the large-cap 1H flips earn the market drift and nothing more in this sample (survivorship-biased). Chandelier 3x is worse than the vector exit (test -0.40pp per trade vs vector, day-clustered t -2.3); 4x and 5x are statistically the same (t -1.4 / -1.5). Slot-limited test book (10 slots, 10% each): vector-only +46.0% maxDD 13.5 pts Sharpe 1.22; chandelier 3x +7.2% / 27.1 / 0.17; 4x +25.5% / 19.6 / 0.68; 5x +51.6% / 13.8 / 1.34 (5x ~ vector, not a clear win). 15m (60 days, one regime): vector -0.27%, chandelier 3x -0.07% (t +1.86 vs vector), all negative before drift; excess ~0. 25bp costs cut the 1H test mean to +0.46% vector / +0.21% chandelier 4x.
report_111 (Rule A replay on NQ 5m, validated corr 0.991 to the logged trades; 346 trades): removing the +75 target helps the mean (2018-21 +8.6 -> +12.3 pts per trade, 2022+ +11.2 -> +13.0) but raises 2022+ max drawdown (600 -> 710 pts); chandelier on top of the -50 stop: 3x +13.5 / +11.5, 4x +10.9 / +13.7, 5x +11.8 / +13.7 with max drawdown 272-331 / 710-823 (worse than base in 2022+); chandelier replacing the stop produces tail trades (-130 to -245 pts) and larger drawdown. No variant beats BASE on mean AND drawdown in both eras: not a lead. Other legs and the eval/payout sim were not replayed (their per-trade paths are not saved), so nothing here changes the saved variant J.

## report_112 note (Nov 15 2026): time of year and liquidity rotation (daily flips, 2019-Mar 2026, train 2019-21 / test 2022+, 30bp; large-cap subset dv >= $100M, px >= $10)
SEASON: no stable calendar effect. Winter (Nov-Apr) vs summer (May-Oct) flips: train +4.31% vs +4.49%, test -0.53% vs +0.33% (opposite to the declared sign in test; the month-clustered t came out NaN because the winter/summer month table did not align, so no t is claimed, differences are tiny). Month means rank-correlate +0.66 train vs test on all names but +0.03 for large caps; individual months (May +15.4% / +2.5%, Nov +10.5% / +3.2%, Feb -2.5% / -4.1%) are fat-tail noise. ROTATION (other names in vector bear at the prior close): high bear share flips do better (train +11.8% vs +2.2% for low; test +0.88% vs -0.93%, IC +0.04 / +0.05), but the control shows it is market timing, not flip selection: the equal-weight universe's own forward 20-day return also rises with bear share (train +5.5% -> +8.9%, test -1.2% -> +2.9%), the top-minus-bottom month-clustered t is undefined because high- and low-bear-share regimes do not share months (2020 crash rebound dominates train; large-cap top quintile train +38% on n 115), and in large caps test IC is +0.011. SECTOR rotation (same SIC2 share in bear) has no effect (IC -0.010 / +0.019, month-t +0.46 / -0.33; large caps train +4.2pp t +1.9 but test -2.7pp). Not a lead.

## report_113 note (Nov 15 2026): 'March-July, out in February' (daily flips, train 2019-21 / test 2022-26, 30bp; year-paired differences because every calendar month is wholly in or out)
Window chosen after seeing the report_112 month table, so data-snooped by construction. Mar-Jul only does NOT hold: all names test -0.14% in-window vs -0.06% outside (year-paired -0.22pp t -0.12, 2 of 4 years positive); large caps train +11.2% vs -1.7% (year-paired +12.9pp, t +1.0, 2020 = +37.5pp) but test -1.17% vs -0.47% (-1.77pp, t -0.91, 1 of 4 years). Placebo over all 12 five-month rotations: Mar-Jul ranks 8th of 12 on test (all names); the best rotations (Aug-Dec, Sep-Jan, Jul-Nov) are +1.3 to +3.6pp in test but -1 to -12pp in train for large caps. Skipping February only: all-names test non-Feb minus Feb +4.64pp, 4 of 5 years positive, t +2.18 (flips in February -4.14% vs +0.29% otherwise); large caps test +4.78pp, 4 of 5 years, t +2.00; train is positive in 2 of 3 years but dominated by 2020 (+25.8pp) with 2021 negative (-4.8pp) and 2019 flat. February flips were negative in both eras (train -2.5%, test -4.1%; large caps -0.3% / -5.2%). One month picked from 12 after the fact; no mechanism tested (a January-rally-then-fade story is a guess). Lead, not a rule.

## report_114 note (Nov 15 2026): December / Dec-Jan (Rey's actual idea; earlier model 12-1, mechanism = year-end unloading). Correction: report_113's February result answered a misread; December was the intended month. Daily flips, train 2019-21 / test 2022-25, 30bp, year-paired differences.
DEC flips are below the rest of the year in both eras: all names train -1.87% vs +4.89% (year-paired -3.86pp, t -2.46, 3 of 3 years negative); test +0.73% vs -0.07% (-0.80pp, t -0.31, 2 of 4 years negative: 2022 +3.5, 2023 +3.8, 2024 -4.9, 2025 -5.6). Large caps: train -4.38% vs +4.68% (-11.1pp, t -1.4, driven by 2020), test -4.31% vs -0.31% (-3.75pp, t -1.43, 2024 -6.1 and 2025 -10.1). DEC-JAN (12-1): all names train -3.35pp / test -1.81pp (t -0.57 / -0.90); large caps train -8.6pp / test -3.98pp (t -0.76 / -1.83). JAN alone: train mixed (2020 -18.8), test -2.6pp (-3.5pp large caps). Within December: first half weak in both eras (all names -0.47% train, -1.33% test; large caps -2.9% / -4.3%); second half flips sign (train -3.36%, test +6.30%, n 331, huge share 1.21%), so the 'unloading' story fits the first half only; that split is post hoc. Placebo (single-month year-paired, worst first): December ranks 3rd worst of 12 in train and 5th worst in test for all names (7th/4th for large caps) with other months (Feb, Mar, Jul) equally weak, so it is not unique. Lead, not a rule: sign is consistent in both eras but test significance is weak (t -0.3 all names, -1.4 large caps).

## report_115/115b note (Nov 15 2026): which flip classes are worth trading (daily flips, 30,323; 30 pre-declared cells = price bucket x frozen-score tier x flip-bar volume; selection on train 2019-21, judged on test 2022-26)
The declared selection rule (train mean > 0 and ex-top-3 > 0, n >= 80) FAILED the lead bar: train was a +4.4% tailwind so it kept 15 of 19 eligible cells and the union equalled all flips on test (-0.21% vs -0.09%, permutation share 0.35). Large-cap grid: 1 of 4 eligible cells selected, test -0.53% vs -0.77%, permutation 0.49 (large-cap flip classes carry no edge). The class map itself shows the structure: only cells with price < $5 stay positive from train into test (2-5/top20/vol>=1.5: train +42.8% -> test +12.5%, ex3 +4.4%, n 355; <2/top20/vol>=1.5 +33.9% -> +17.3%, n 88; 2-5/mid40/vol<1.5 +13.7% -> +5.5%, n 334); $10+ cells are ~0 or negative in test in every tier. POST-HOC follow-up (report_115b, written after seeing test numbers, so a shortlist not a result): price < $5 alone: test +6.97% (ex3 +4.87%, ~327 flips/yr, 4 of 5 years positive); price < $5 AND frozen score top 20%: train +33.9% (2020 = +69.5, 2019 -4.5), test +10.51% (ex3 +6.46%, huge 5.97%, median -9.75%, 171/yr; 2022 -1.4, 2023 +11.8, 2024 +14.3, 2025 +12.0, 2026 +8.9), 100bp +9.81%; price < $5 and (top 20% or VOLX >= 1.5): test +9.22% (199/yr). Caveats: survivorship (sub-$5 names still listed today) flatters this most, thin fills, median trade loses ~10%, one-year-dominated train (2020). Use as the shadow-ledger shortlist.

## report_116 note (Nov 15 2026): confirmations besides flip-bar volume (daily flips with features, 30,080; universes A all, B price < $5, C price < $5 & score top 20%; train 2019-21 / test 2022-26)
Nothing PASSED the declared rule (declared sign on IC and tercile spread in both eras, test month-t >= 2): F1 close location, F3 gap, F4 near 52-week high, F5 pre-flip volume trend, F6 above 200d SMA, F7 20d relative strength, F8 chop, F9 base length, F10 breadth (month-t undefined, regimes share no months) are inconsistent or insignificant. Two features separate the tail in both eras with the opposite of a clean pass, and I am NOT re-signing them: F2 flip-bar range/ATR (expansion): in B the top tercile beats the bottom by +44pp (t +1.3) train and +11.4pp (t +2.17) test, in C +35.7pp / +11.9pp; its Spearman IC to net return is negative in both eras because the IC reads the median (the same artifact as report_90), while IC to the >= +10% flag is positive (+0.076 / +0.074 in B). F11 distance of the flip close above the vector level (declared '-' tight ratchet is better, which is WRONG in daily): wide break beats tight in both eras (B: +47.5pp t +1.4 train, +13.0pp t +2.94 test; C: +46.7pp / +20.6pp t +2.14; all flips +10.9pp t +2.0 / +1.2pp), and the IC to the >= +10% flag is +0.17 / +0.15 in B. Together they say the SIZE OF THE BREAK (how far and how wide the flip bar goes past the level) is the confirmation, not tightness; this is post hoc (sign reversed after seeing results) and needs the shadow ledger / fresh data before use. F4 near-high is the wrong sign in train (far below the 252d high did better: +11.5% vs -0.2% all flips), insignificant in test; F7 leaders vs laggards flips sign across eras on mean but is positive on spread in test in B/C (t +1.5 / +1.4).

## report_117 note (Nov 15 2026): what catches outliers (target BIG = net >= +30%, HUGE = >= +100%; AUC per feature, train 2019-21 vs test 2022-26; BIG base rate 8.7% train / 5.9% test, HUGE 2.1% / 1.1%)
Consistent outlier catchers (|AUC-0.5| >= 0.04, same direction both eras, all flips): ATRP (daily ATR/price; AUC big 0.739 / 0.652, huge 0.847 / 0.779; top-decile BIG 23.0% / 13.2% vs bottom 0.8% / 1.9%), EXT (close/60d low - 1; 0.685 / 0.610, huge 0.794 / 0.728; top-decile BIG 18.7% / 13.7% vs 2.1% / 3.2%), LTURN (log turnover; 0.611 / 0.601), LMC (log market cap, low; 0.371 / 0.397), LPX (low price; 0.398 / 0.418), LDV (low dollar volume; 0.454 / 0.450), F4 (far BELOW the 252d high; 0.323 / 0.440), F7 (20d relative strength +; 0.569 / 0.568), F2 (flip-bar range/ATR +; 0.545 / 0.568), F10 (low breadth; 0.447 / 0.450), frozen score lr (0.598 / 0.627). Inside price < $5 the same set holds (ATRP 0.708 / 0.625, EXT 0.676 / 0.609, LTURN 0.569 / 0.610, LMC 0.361 / 0.426, F2 0.562 / 0.596, F7 0.542 / 0.557, F4 0.377 / 0.448); flip-bar volume VOLX is only 0.525 / 0.581 there. These mostly restate one thing: tail size lives in small, volatile, high-turnover names that are extended off a low. Combined rank-sum in price < $5 on test: top 20% captures 43.1% of HUGE and 30.3% of BIG (precision 18.5% vs 12.2% base; mean +18.5% vs +7.0%; ex-top3 +8.6%, median -13.1%). CAVEAT: the consistency screen used test-era AUC, so those test numbers are optimistic; only the directions are a finding. Not a trading rule; supports adding ATRP, EXT, turnover and market cap to the live scan and the shadow ledger.

## report_118 note (Nov 15 2026): returns of the outlier model OS = train-ECDF ranks of ATRP + EXT + log turnover (cut = train 80th pct, 2.193; 25,519 of 30,080 flips eligible, rest lack shares data); TEST 2022-01..2026-02, additive, entry next open, exit open after the bear flip
Per trade (30bp): OS top20 all names test +2.85% (ex3 +1.75%, vs frozen score top20 +2.09%); OS top20 & price < $5: train +67.7% (n 209), test +12.96% (n 504, ~122/yr, ex3 +7.42%, huge 7.54%, median -10.18%, positive every year: 2022 +2.5, 2023 +16.5, 2024 +10.4, 2025 +17.6, 2026 +9.9; 100bp +12.26%) vs frozen score top20 & price < $5 test +11.40% (2022 -0.2). Price < $5 alone +7.12%. Book (5% per position, 30bp): OS & px<5 N=10 +72.2% over the window (~+17.5%/yr, maxDD -49.6 pts, Sharpe 0.49, 180 trades, 8.9 avg positions), N=20 +125.8% (+30.4%/yr, maxDD -64.5, Sharpe 0.55); frozen score & px<5 N=10 +71.5% (maxDD -40.3, Sharpe 0.58), N=20 +134.0% (maxDD -83.0, Sharpe 0.59); px<5 random N=20 +123.3% (-62.6, 0.64); OS top 10% & px<5 N=20 +202.9% (maxDD -112.5, Sharpe 0.73); all flips ~0. At 100bp the books lose ~5-15 points. The OS model does not beat the frozen score on book Sharpe: both are the same under-$5 supply tail (consistency screen used test AUC, so the OS numbers are optimistic). 1/N sizing doubles the drawdown (OS & px<5 N=10 +144% / -99 pts). Returns are additive on starting capital, survivorship-flattered, first-come slot noise large, thin-fill costs unmodeled.

## report_119/120 notes (Nov 15 2026): slot design for the sub-$5 book; transfer of the outlier features to large caps
report_119 (TEST 2022-01..2026-02, 5% each, 30bp; baseline first-come reproduces report_118: OS & px<5 N=10 +72.4% / maxDD -49.6 / Sharpe 0.49, N=20 +126.1% / -64.5 / 0.56). QUEUE (waiting flips fill freed slots, best score first, entry at the prior close, K = 3/5/10 days): OS pool N=10 +80.6 / +87.0 / +97.9% (Sharpe 0.52 / 0.56 / 0.56), N=20 +182.8 / +168.8 / +206.5% (Sharpe 0.75 / 0.69 / 0.77) and it survives removing the top 3 pool winners (N=20 K=10 +167% Sharpe 0.66 vs first-come +90% / 0.41); BUT it is not robust across pools: frozen-score pool N=10 K=3 +16.5% (Sharpe 0.15), K=5 +37.6% (0.31), K=10 +86.2% (0.65) vs first-come +71.6% (0.58), and by year it is worse than first-come in 3 of 5 years at N=10 (small margins), so the declared lead bar (beat on Sharpe and total, not worse in more than one year) is not met. REPLACE (swap the lowest-score position when a higher-score flip appears): N=10 +172.8% (Sharpe 0.73) is one trade: 2024 +141 points came from a single multi-thousand-percent flip that first-come missed; removing it gives +62.6% (Sharpe 0.39) vs first-come +72.4%; for the frozen-score pool the margin never triggers (scores span 0.1-0.3). TIER RESERVE (5 of 10 slots for the top 10% OS) = first-come to the decimal. Conclusion: slot breadth N drives the book (N=5 Sharpe 0.35, N=10 0.49, N=20 0.56); no slot policy is robustly better; queue K=5-10 is the only candidate and needs the ledger. 
report_120: large-cap daily flips (dv >= $100M, px >= $10): OS quintiles worked in train (top +7.1% vs bottom -0.1%, win10 17.3% vs 10.7%; mid-cap train +10.9% vs -1.1%, win10 27.2% vs 7.5%) and do NOT hold in test (large caps -0.60% vs -0.92%, mid -0.30% vs -0.59%; win10 AUC 0.54 / 0.56, directionally right but flat on means). Spearman IC to net is negative on both eras (the median artifact again). Large-cap 1H flips (7,190, 2023-12..2026-11) with daily ATRP: top tercile beats bottom in both eras (net +1.20% vs +0.24% train, +1.23% vs +0.18% test; win10 11-15% vs 5%; drift-excess +0.42% vs -0.15% / -0.23%); test day-clustered top-minus-bottom +1.74pp t +2.49 (train +0.78pp t +1.25); EXT alone +0.36pp t +0.78 (not significant) and ATRP+EXT +0.80pp t +1.51, so only the volatility component transfers. Short window, bull market, hold ~49 bars (7 sessions); a lead for the 1H large-cap screener, not a rule.

## report_121 note (Nov 15 2026): STEP C regime identifiers for NQ legs (fit on train <=2021 only, features lagged 1 day)
Rule-based state machine (vol expansion ATR5/ATR60 with hysteresis x 10d trend efficiency; also volume EMA ratio, overnight-inventory share, level as features for GMM/HMM). Metric: for each of ~13 legs/models take the train best and worst state, measure the same spread on test; null = circular-shifted state series. Results (legs with test spread>0 / sum t / p): own SM 6-state 8/13, +4.6, p .15; own SM vol-only 3-state 10/14, +6.0, p .09; GMM K=4 6/12, +0.1, p .49; HMM 3-state 5/13, +2.5, p .27. Only RA (quiet beats expansion, t +2.2) and ICT-strong / V15a (expansion beats quiet) transfer cleanly; the vol-only machine is best and borderline, GMM/HMM are noise. No gate adopted yet.

## report_122 note (Nov 15 2026): real Wyckoff on $1-5 small caps, alone and with the vector (definitions fixed before results; train 2019-21 / test 2022+)
Event = 40d range (<=12 ATR) + spring (pierce 20d low, close back) + dry-up (10d vol <=0.8x range vol) + SOS (close above range high on >=1.5x volume). FULL event n 14 / 73 only; 20d excess vs same-month $1-5 average +0.95% / +1.17% (t +0.3 / -0.3), medians negative. SOS alone -1.7% / -0.3% (t -1.6 / -1.4). By vector state: no pattern (n 8-34 cells). With vector (flips to bear exit): flip bar = SOS 23.5% / 11.1% vs all $1-5 flips 36.6% / 7.0%, not better; FULL Wyckoff flips n 4 / 14, no edge. Verdict: no edge as strictly defined; sample too thin to rule out the discretionary version. Looser definitions would be post-hoc.

## reports 123-126 (Nov 15 2026): extraction pass, "find a signal that adds"
123/124 vector-history features on daily flips (declared: short prior bear leg = shakeout [+], shallow bear depth [+], market flip health [+], breadth thrust [+], whipsaw count [-]; train terciles): ALL FAIL on $1-5 and score-top-20 universes (spreads flip sign between eras; the 2019-21 $1-5 sample is dominated by 2020, n 260 and a few huge winners). The vector's own history adds nothing beyond ATRP/EXT/turnover. Shakeout (spring-by-vector) is the Wyckoff idea from report_122 and also fails.
125 one frozen ridge classifier (17 train-ECDF features, target BIG>=30% / HUGE>=100%, train only): test AUC big 0.65, huge 0.78 on all flips, equal to the 3-feature OS (0.64/0.76). Top 20% of all flips catches 35-36% of BIG and 57-59% of HUGE in test; inside px<5 the frozen score (lr) top 20% (26% of flips) catches 36% of BIG / 48% of HUGE, OS 23% / 35%. More features do not raise the ceiling. OS on 1H penny flips (60d, select Aug20-Oct5 / judge Oct6-Nov12): top20 is WORSE (-6.2% vs -3.2%), dead.
126 NQ: report_121 vol-state machine used as a RA sizer. RA sized pts/trade EXP -1.6 / -0.8 (train/test), QUIET +11.3 / +14.7, MID +0.4 / +16.2 (hypothesis was the opposite; RA is the quiet-day leg). Tilts (x1.5 QUIET, x0.5 EXP): all-legs pts/day +2.52 -> +2.88 (train), +5.67 -> +6.14 (test) but worst day -88 -> -128 / -135 -> -141. Skip RA in EXP: pts/day +2.64 / +5.72, worst day test -118 vs -135, sd 39.7 -> 38.7: small risk-only gain, both eras. Vol-scaled RA stop/target (S=ATR14d/train median, clip .7-2.0), judged in R: EXP test R +0.15 -> +0.04 (worse), no gain. Prop eval/payout simulation NOT rerun (per-leg paths not saved).

## report_127 note (Nov 15 2026): context look-back model (last K matured trades of same stock / SIC2 sector / cap-x-price cell / market-breadth regime, plus 100-NN analog), strictly point-in-time (a flip joins a history only after its exit)
Trade-outcome look-backs have no predictive power: AUC for BIG 0.42-0.54 in test (0.30-0.45 in 2020-21, i.e. INVERTED: recent peer winners preceded worse flips in the 2019-21 boom), IC ~0, tercile spreads small and sign-unstable. Reason (power): BIG base rate is 6-9%, so 5-20 prior trades hold ~0.5-1.5 big events; the mean is a lottery-tail estimator. KNN analog in rank-feature space (adaptive, refit monthly on matured flips only) reaches test AUC big 0.637 / huge 0.736 (all) and 0.606 / 0.663 (px<5), equal to or below the frozen ridge (0.656/0.777 and 0.617/0.690); a 50/50 rank blend adds nothing on all flips and +0.1pp AUC / +3pts huge-catch on px<5 (noise level). Conclusion: the static features carry the signal; context-specific retraining or history feeding does not add. Sector/cap/regime heterogeneity is already priced by ATRP, mcap, turnover, price.

## report_128 note (Nov 15 2026): enter at the vector trigger intraday instead of next open (daily-bar approximation, 2019-26 full universe, px<20)
Trigger T = level + 0.35 ATR (prior bar); entry max(open, T); true flips hold to usual exit; false crosses (close back under) exit at that close; 30bp. TEST 2022+ px 1-5: 3,654 crosses, only 41% true flips; true flips enter 3.65pp cheaper (+9.9% vs +6.2%), false crosses lose -3.2% each; per cross +2.2%, total 8,075pp vs 9,395pp for next-open on true flips only. Top-20% ATRP+EXT (known at prior close), px<5: test 979 crosses / 393 true: total +4,795pp vs +5,033pp baseline (true +18.5% vs +12.8%, false -4.25%); train +14,822pp vs +13,119pp. So no robust gain, and 2.5x the entries (small-cap spreads at a stop-buy trigger not in the 30bp). Late-day entry (flip bar close) remains the only cheap lateness fix (+0.3pp in 2019-21, none 2022+).

## reports 129-131 (Nov 15 2026): take-profit vs move flips, weekly nesting, ratchet shape, pattern library, flip correlation, sub-$1
129 (daily flips 30k, declared signs, train terciles; pass = same sign + test |t|>=2): WEEKLY NESTING fails (W_BULL train weekly-bear flips better +7.9% vs +1.0%, test reverse; sign flips). RATCHET SHAPE: retrace of bear leg / break above leg start / ratchet slope / downtrend context all fail on test (train-significant for retrace and break, t +2.8/+3.5, but test t +1.4). PATTERN LIBRARY (k=10 on 40 normalized pre-flip closes): train-best clusters invert in test. BUY/SELL VOLUME: AD5 and bear-leg accumulation fail. PASS in test: VRB (flip-bar volume / mean volume of the prior bear leg) t +2.0 all, +2.8 px<5; RES (flip-day return minus market, idiosyncratic) t +3.5 px<5 (train same sign, t +1.3); PEERS (fewer same-sector flips) t -2.7 all. Correlation with market (CORR60) fails. Composite 'MOVE' (no downtrend & buying) vs 'TP' (downtrend & selling) does not separate (TP n 13-77, equal or better in test). Incremental check: adding RES+VRB to the 5-feature base (ATRP, EXT, turnover, mcap, price) changes test AUC by 0.000-0.006 -> redundant (RES corr .5 with ATRP, .62 with F2). So the take-profit/move split is not separable with the daily proxies tried; the volume-vs-bear-leg and idiosyncratic-move features carry its mechanism but add nothing beyond ATRP/EXT/turnover.
130/131 sub-$1: wide universe has only 36 sub-$1 flips (it requires >=$1 at entry to the universe), so it cannot answer; lowpx_daily (2,024 names, 2 yrs, survivorship + currently-low-price selection) gives 142 flips: select Jun25-Feb26 mean +54% (22% huge), judge Mar-Nov26 -9% (1.3% huge); judge half has 6 BIG and 1 HUGE events. ATRP is the only feature with the same sign in both halves (AUC big .62/.78) but n is far too small; no conclusion. Need a survivorship-free sub-$1 history (delisted names) with a multi-year split.

## report_132 note (Nov 15 2026): residual programme - what ATRP/EXT/turnover leaves unanswered, and other model families (px<5 daily flips, train 2019-21 / test 2022+)
Q1 false positives: test top-20% of the BIG model among px<5 (n 271): win 28%, big30 17%, 59% lose >10%. The score picks the fat-tailed population, not winners. Q2 misses: 38 of 58 test HUGE flips rank outside the top 20%; missed ones have lower extension (median 0.39 vs 0.81 caught) and lower ATRP (0.060 vs 0.086) but similar volume/break size. Separate models per extension tercile (low/mid/high, ridge on 17 features, train only) do NOT beat the global one (pooled test AUC big 0.604 vs 0.617 px<5; 0.644 vs 0.656 all). Q3 level gap (2019-21 vs 2022+): not explained by feature differences.
Families (declared signs, pass = same sign + test |t|>=2): AMT/overhead supply (OH share of 252d volume above the close; OH30 within +30%), ICT fair-value-gap displacement (FVG), FINRA short-sale-volume ratio on the flip day / change vs 20d / prior 5d: ALL FAIL on B and C; incremental test AUC over the 5-feature base: +0.000 to +0.008 (BIG), -0.015 to 0.000 (HUGE). Combined with 122 (Wyckoff) and 129 (weekly nesting, ratchet shape, pattern library, buy/sell volume), 13F (85) and the 8-K catalyst file (only 2025+, 321 tickers: not testable on a split), the vector-flip quality signal remains supply/volatility (price, ATRP, extension, turnover, mcap). Correction to narrative: institutional positioning did NOT survive (13F, insider lumpy); what survived is supply/physics.

## report_133 note (Nov 15 2026): PHYSICS / EXTREME-VALUE MODELS of the flip payoff (fit 2019-21 by MLE in torch, judged 2022+)
Theory: a flip is a diffusion position with a trailing drawdown stop; for Brownian motion with drift mu, vol sigma and drawdown stop b the max at the stop is Exponential (Taylor 1975); unobserved drift heterogeneity makes the unconditional peak a mixture of exponentials = heavy tail. Modelled L = log(1+PEAK) (max excursion during hold), not noisy net return. RESULTS: (1) Mixture-of-exponentials with covariate-dependent scale and mixing wins held-out log-likelihood (+0.068 nats/flip over constant GPD; plain exponential(x) is -0.016, GPD(x) +0.052, GPD with xi(x) +0.055): the 'real move vs noise' drift mixture is the right shape. (2) DIFFUSIVE SCALING holds: elasticity of excursion scale to ATRP = 0.98 (theory 1). (3) TAIL INDEX: alpha ~1.5-2.3 overall; Hill alpha by supply bucket is stable across eras and ordered the same way: low price 1.03 / 1.17 (train/test) vs high price 1.40 / 1.54; low mcap 1.02 / 1.15 vs 1.51 / 1.58; high turnover 1.07 / 1.20 vs low 1.39 / 1.50. Supply changes the tail EXPONENT (alpha near 1 => infinite mean: book returns are set by the few extremes), not just the scale. (4) Level gap 2019-21 vs 2022+: const-GPD xi 0.645 (alpha 1.55) -> 0.526 (alpha 1.90): the tail got thinner; scale unchanged (0.0765 -> 0.0778). (5) RANKING px<5 test: AUC peak>=+100% 0.742-0.746 (exp/mix/GPD) vs 0.705 logistic; net>=+100% 0.707-0.714 vs 0.690; top-20% catches 43-48% of net +100% flips vs 29% for the logistic; no gain on all flips (AUC 0.78 either way). Calibration of the mixture/GPD tail probability is good in the top deciles (pred 0.19 / actual 0.13 top decile; monotone). (6) Slot books (px<5, test, 5%/slot, top 20% by score): N=20 totals +219..+232% (ex top-3 trades +83..+96%) for the physics models vs +190% (OS), +206% (logistic), +100% (frozen lr; ex-top3 +52..+72%, -1% for lr at N=10); N=10 is inconsistent (M1 +63%, M0 +172%, OS +165%) so the book gain is suggestive, not proven. Caveats: survivorship-biased universe; peak is not the exit return; differences between rankers are within the lumpy-payoff noise.

## report_134 note (Nov 15 2026): exit width by predicted tail class (physics prediction: tight stop for noise-state names, wide for real-state names) - FALSIFIED
Declared policy: top-20% predicted P(+100%) (mixture M1, train cut) -> vector exit; rest -> chandelier M=3 (vector backstop). Test 2022+ mean per trade: px<5 declared +4.45% vs uniform vector exit +7.33% (uniform c5 +6.14%, c3 +1.72%); ALL declared +0.03% vs vector +0.23%. Even the REST group earns more under the vector exit (px<5 test +4.5% vs c3 +1.0%, c2 +0.8%): the tail is not confined to the high-score names, and any chandelier tighter than the vector clips it. Tight stops do improve median and win rate (px<5 test: win 26% -> 33-36%, median -6.9% -> -3.4..-5.2%) at a 2-3pp cost in mean. Conclusion: the vector's own ratchet is already near the right width; the exit equation does not give extra alpha; a tighter rest-group stop is a variance/win-rate tool only. Remaining physics lever: position sizing from the tail index (alpha ~1.0-1.9), and a trailing tail-index regime signal.

## report_135 note (Nov 15 2026): sizing from the tail, physics regime variables (Hill tail index, Hawkes), gating; tail diagnostics on large caps and the prop legs
SIZING (px<5, top-20% M1P, N=20, test): equal +225% (ex-top3 +89%, maxDD 38, monthly Sharpe 0.97); vol-target 1/ATRP +220% (+102%, 45, 1.09); conviction P(+100%) +255% (+82%, 33, 0.84); vol x conviction +244% (+97%, 39, 0.99). Differences are inside the lumpy-payoff noise; no sizing rule wins on all of total / ex-top3 / DD / Sharpe. (Growth-optimal leverage scan hit its 6x cap on every rule: survivorship-flattered returns, ignore the leverage numbers.) REGIME: rolling 12m Hill index only has 32 valid months (train has 1: not testable on a split); Hawkes (Poisson, exp kernel) on daily universe flip counts, trailing 252d: branching ratio 0.30-0.59 (mean 0.47) = moderate flip clustering, but month-level Spearman with next-month selected-flip mean is -0.14 train / -0.12 test and with BIG rate +0.02 / +0.01; excitation lambda/mu +0.33 (8 months) / +0.07 test. GATES (skip months in the bad tercile cut from train): branch gate never triggers in test; alpha gate and heat gate cut the test book from +225% to about +100% (they remove profitable months): reject. This repeats the earlier flip-contagion / PEERS / BURST results: aggregate clustering does not time the book. TAILS elsewhere: large-cap 1H flips (7,190, 730d) mean +0.64%, median -1.7%, win 26%, skew 9.2, Hill alpha wins 1.53 / losses 2.1, top-5% of trades = 271% of total (same lottery profile as small caps); 15m (2,175) mean -0.27%, alpha wins 1.55. Prop legs (sized pts): RA alpha 2.45 wins / 2.25 losses (symmetric heavy), M1 2.17 / 4.47, R1 1.63 / 1.35, ON+ 1.69 / 2.85, ON- 1.88 / 3.47, V15a 1.56 / 3.97, RULES 6.96 / 6.91 (thin: top 5% of trades = 33% of pts; the steady leg).

### CORRECTION to report 151 (Nov 15 2026, found while building report 152)
M1, R1 and RULES trade lists have NO direction column; report 151 treated them as all-long, so their IPDA discount/premium, draw, NDOG/NWOG rows for those legs are INVALID (the 'cross-leg discount finding' stated above for M1 and RULES is withdrawn). Only RA and V15a carry a true direction. Direction was then inferred from the 5m price path (xs/infer_leg_dir.py: matches 100% / ~98% of the trades it can decide on RA / V15a, but decides only 29-52% of M1/R1/RULES trades, and those are mostly trades that exit at price targets = selection toward winners, so the subset level is inflated: M1 +26.7/+48.7 vs +8.5/+9.1 full).
Corrected result (report_151b_legs_fixed.py): M1 DISC40 +21.4/+48.6 vs DRAW40 +31.3/+48.8; DISC20 +19.1/+36.4 vs DRAW20 +33.4/+58.3 -> no discount advantage (draw is >= discount). RULES DISC40 +4.2/+15.8 vs DRAW40 +7.1/+14.4 -> none. Clean legs: RA DISC40 +14.1/+20.4 vs DRAW40 +1.1/+4.6 (n 68/67 vs 32/53, PASS at 20/40/60d); V15a DISC40 +9.2/+40.7 vs DRAW40 +9.3/+17.3 (mixed: DRAW20 PASS, DISC20 +1.6/+33.5). IFVG (own direction) DISC40 +4.6/+2.5 vs +2.2/+3.4 (weak). => The IPDA premium/discount gate is supported on RA only (n ~135); not a cross-leg result. IFVG results (reports 144-150) are unaffected (they carry their own direction). report_152 (discount gate through Lucid) not run: it needs direction for M1/RULES and the evidence does not justify gating them.

A | B pts/trade: S1 premium fade +0.4 (n229, t0.1) | +41.2 (n194, t3.7, win 61%, PF 2.03); S2 discount fade +2.4 | +4.8 (t 0.3/0.4); S4 loose-VA premium +25.0 (t2.4) | -3.3 (t-0.2); combined +5.3 (t1.2) | +21.4 (t2.9). Wicked-rescue never triggers (0.5 daily ATR stop is ~100+ pts, a 5m bar never reaches it).
FAILS the split (no setup same-sign |t|>=2 both). The 2022+ strength of S1 matches the rise in gap-fill rates in 2022+ (report 154: 30-60 pt gaps filled 55% -> 75%); candidate for a regime-conditioned test, not for deployment. Risk per trade ~100+ NQ pts, too big for a Lucid 50K at 1 ct.

## Reports 159-160 (Nov 15): gates for the S1 premium fade (Rey: find the gate, don't toss it)
S1 = tight prior VA (<= A median) + open above VAH -> short to VAL, stop .5 daily ATR. By year mean pts/trade: 2018 -1.7, 2019 -5.4, 2020 -3.2, 2021 +9.7, 2022 +39.9, 2023 +19.6, 2024 +10.1, 2025 +82.3, 2026 +83.7. Weak/negative through 2020, positive every year since 2021.
12 pre-declared gates tested, cuts frozen on 2018-21: INRANGE (open <= PDH), FILL40 (trailing gap-fill rate), NEARVAH, PDUP, ABOVE20, LOWVOL, GAPUP, TIGHT25, ATRP_HI, RET120_UP, VOL_EXP, POS250_HI. NONE passes (IN group same sign |t|>=2 n>=30 in both eras): the 2018-21 half has no edge in any subset. Directionally consistent but not significant in A: INRANGE IN +12.0 vs OUT -6.4; TIGHT25 IN +10.7 vs OUT -11.2; FILL40 IN +3.4 vs OUT -2.7 (all better IN than OUT in A), but in B the OUT groups are as good or better, so no gate separates regimes. Yearly ATRP does not explain 2018-21 vs 2022+.

## Reports 161-162 (Nov 15): S1 premium fade as a regime model, and in the Lucid 50K sim
Report 161: independent opening-reversion measures show NO break at 2021: gap-fade open->close (ATR units) A +0.003 -> B +0.006 (Welch t 0.09), 09:30-10:00 reversal A +0.013 -> B 0.000, gap-fill rate A 57.4% -> B 59.0%. The S1 effect after 2020 is specific to S1 conditions (tight prior VA + open above VAH), not a market-wide opening-reversion regime. Causal switch reg60 (trailing 60d mean of gap-fade) >0: ON A +3.5 (t .4) | +60.0 (t 3.2); OFF -1.8 | +24.2 (t 1.9). Does not pass in A. S1 stop = 0.5 daily ATR14: median 43 pts in 2018-21, 93 pts in 2022+ (so ~100 pts is right for the regime-on years).
Report 162 (Lucid 50K one account, 2022-25 starts; first run was invalid: the S1 leg was dropped because NAMES lacked it, fixed): variant I + IFVG 0.5 ct = pass 86%, paid once 65%, 1.90 payouts/eval, net $2,825, 32 days to pass. + S1 0.2 ct (2 MNQ): pass 75%, paid 58%, 2.04, net $3,068, 23 days. + S1 0.2 ct regime-ON only: pass 84%, paid 63%, 2.06, net $3,180, 28 days. + S1 0.1 ct: pass 77%, paid 57%, 1.79, net $2,645. 0.3 ct: pass 74%, 1.86, $2,812. 2018-21 starts: no benefit (S1 inactive then): 2.00-2.09 payouts, net $2.0-2.2k.

## Reports 170-172 (Nov 15): where bad stretches come from, payout budget, options greeks gate
Report 170 (deploy config daily P&L, NQ-pt x contract equivalents, 2018-26): beta to NQ daily change +0.001 (corr 0.00); legs nearly market-neutral (ON- +0.28, ON+ +0.14, S1 -0.14, rest ~0). Daily correlation of a leg with the rest of the system: V15a .32, M1 .22, RA .18, RULES .11, IFVG -0.01, S1 .02. 102 twenty-day blocks: 15% negative; bad = bottom 20% (pnl <= +11): mean -31 vs good +159. Environment at block start does NOT separate them on vol (ATRP t -1.8), 20d return, efficiency ratio, gap size, vol expansion. Exceptions: bad blocks sit closer to 250d highs (dd250 -4.2% vs -7.3%, t +2.2) and are less often below the 200d SMA (5% vs 23%, t -2.8); blocks starting below the 200d SMA had 0% negative blocks (+165 avg). Worst blocks: 2023-03 (high vol, no direction, er 0.09), 2024-12, 2021-07 and 2019-04 (calm grind up), 2020-09, 2024-11 (er 0.01), 2023-06 (+10% grind), 2019-07; worst leg ON+ x3, RULES x3, RA, V15a. In bad blocks RA -13 vs +33, ON+ -13 vs +22, V15a -4 vs +30, S1 -6 vs +15, IFVG +6 vs +10 (holds up). Core legs stall together; IFVG is the diversifier.
Report 171: one account at a time, 2018-26 (1,993 trading days): variant I alone 40 payouts (5.1/yr); deploy 51 (6.4/yr). Time in eval 58% -> 52%; median days to pass 44 -> 25; funded accounts 20 -> 25, median funded life 28 -> 34 days, ~1.1 payouts per funded month; 6 of 25 funded accounts never paid. Sim ends a funded account when lost or after 5 payouts (graduated).
Report 172 (QQQ daily net vanna / charm, prior day): S1 vanna low third (cuts frozen on 2018-21) +24.3 (t 2.6, n76) | +35.0 (t 3.4, n114) PASS, but raw net vanna is non-stationary (median -28bn 2018 -> -96bn 2024) so the raw cut is partly a time proxy; stationary versions (trailing-250d rank<=1/3: +10.9 t1.9 | +23.4 t1.7; z<-0.5: +11.1 t2.0 | +24.6 t1.9) are positive in both eras, just under the t>=2 bar; high-vanna S1 flips sign (A -23.8 | B +52.5). IFVG: all vanna/charm sign splits pass (vanna>0 +6.2/+3.7, vanna<=0 +2.5/+2.5), no discrimination.

Report 171 funded-phase policies (deploy config, one account at a time, 2018-26; payouts, paid $/yr, funded accounts lost/graduated, median funded life): F0 x1 (current) -> see log; F1 clip(c,.4,1) 60 payouts (7.6/yr), $9,637/yr, lost 10 / graduated 9, life 56d, 3.2 payouts/account; F2 clip(c,.5,1.5) 53 (6.7/yr), $8,431/yr; F3 half after a payout until c>=.75 57 (7.2/yr), $9,278/yr, life 59d; F4 clip(c,.3,1.25) 56 (7.1/yr), $8,826/yr. De-risking the funded phase when hurt (F1) lifts payouts from 6.4 to 7.6/yr and cuts the time spent in evals to 37%; 9 of 19 funded accounts graduate (5 payouts).

## Report 174 (Nov 15): options gates INSIDE the 0DTE era (cuts on 2022-05..2023-12, judged 2024-01..2025-12-15) (xs/report_174_options_post0dte.py)
Rey's point: 2018-21 is the wrong yardstick for options-flow features; 0DTE hedging flow only exists since 2022. Walk-forward inside the regime. S1 premium fade base W1 +23.9 (t 1.4, n66) | W2 +48.2 (t 3.1, n107). Vanna low third +42.2 (t1.8, n22) | +38.3 (t2.9, n75); mid third -25.9 | +51.6; ROOM>0.5ATR to the call/put wall +18.6 (t.9) | +63.2 (t3.2) vs <=0.5 ATR +33.1 | +12.8; NEAR-wall -13.1 | +23.9. IFVG base +2.2 (t1.5, n169) | +4.4 (t3.3, n202); ROOM<=0.5ATR +3.5 (t1.6) | +7.7 (t3.9); NEAR a wall +2.3 | +4.7 (t3.0); charm>0 +4.4 (t1.8) | +6.5 (t1.8); z>0.5 +0.7 | +9.4. NO gate passes (relaxed n>=20, |t|>=2 both windows); several are same-sign in both windows but W1 cells have 20-60 trades. Verdict: promising leads (S1 low vanna, IFVG near-wall), not enough in-regime data to size on; shadow-track live (need daily vanna/charm/walls logged with each signal) and revisit after ~60 more S1 / ~100 more IFVG trades. Also: S1 itself strengthened in 2024-25 (W2 +48 vs W1 +24), consistent with 0DTE intensifying.

- DEPLOY: 84% 63% 2.06 $3,180 | 94% 80% 1.97 $2,165
- S1 only if <=ON VWAP: 83% 60% 1.87 $2,824 | 93% 79% 1.96 $1,951 (worse: gate throws away trades)

- 184 (5m) and 187 (1m redo): 57 configs (anchors ASIA, NY5, NY15, start-of-move; fib 0.5/0.618/0.705/0.79 retrace x targets 0/1/2 leg-SD; extension fades 1.5/2/2.5 W). 1 PASS of 57 (null 0.1): ASIA EXT 2.0 fade with a NEGATIVE sign (-4.5 | -6.7). TP machine full (down R3 / near goal R1) $3,696 | $2,472; near-goal only $3,836 | $2,363; down only $3,723 | $2,508; fixed R1 $3,472 | $2,675; fixed R3 $2,741 | $2,291. ## Reports 189-190 (Nov 15, 21:5x): FIG-V (IFVG) FILL-BAR AUDIT. IMPORTANT CORRECTION to reports 144-188 FIG-V results
Found while building an example trade for Rey: the 5m sim in report 144 starts at the FILL bar and credits a target hit on the whole bar range although the limit fills at an unknown time inside the bar (example 2024-03-12 13:15 short at 18379.1: the bar opened below the 2R target, low printed first, recorded +27.25).
Report 189 (xs/report_189_figv_fillbar_fix.py, 1m bars, fill = first minute through the limit by 0.25, stop-before-target within a minute): 635 of 656 trades refill (21 do not). Original on the same trades +2.93 (t2.8) | +2.83 (t2.9) PASS; 1m fill-resolved +1.36 (t1.3, win 41%) | +0.99 (t1.1, win 40%) FAIL; with 0.5 pt stop slippage +1.06 | +0.69. 105 of 635 trades change result (67 win->loss, 38 loss->win). Shorts +1.8 (t1.3) | +2.6 (t2.0); longs +0.8 | -1.0.
Report 190 (Lucid 50K, 2022-25 | 2018-21 starts): COMBO FIG-V original 90% pass, 72% paid, $3,854 | 97%, 87%, $2,496; FIG-V on 1m 78%, 57%, 2.12.. net $2,492 | 97%, 83%, $2,375; FIG-V 1m + slippage $2,379 | $2,386; COMBO WITHOUT FIG-V 82%, 61%, $3,119 | 94%, 78%, $2,082. DEPLOY no gates: original $3,180 | $2,165, 1m $2,338 | $2,408, without $2,969 | $1,956.
=> FIG-V as corrected does not pass the strict split and is worse than not trading it in 2022-25 in the sim (slightly positive 2018-21). Reports 144-151, 164-168, 175-188 FIG-V and combo numbers inherit the bias. The three gates (177) were tuned on the biased baseline. NOT yet audited: the same fill-bar rule in other 5m sims (variant-I legs RA/M1/R1/ON/RULES/V15a entries and stops); S1 enters at the open (market) so its entry-bar path is valid.
Deploy spec: do NOT deploy FIG-V until re-derived on 1m; revisit gates.

## Report 191 (Nov 15): retest of the core algos after the fill-bar fix
Code read: vector legs (V15a), M1 and RA enter at the NEXT 5m bar OPEN after the signal bar closes (market), stop checked from the entry bar (valid), exits at next open; cost 1.0 pt. So the fill-bar bug is specific to limit-order models (IFVG); core legs unchanged. Lucid numbers with FIG-V removed (report 190): COMBO without FIG-V 82% pass / 61% paid / 2.06 / $3,119 (2022-25) | 94% / 78% / 1.80 / $2,082 (2018-21); no-gate deploy without FIG-V 79% / 52% / 1.90 / $2,969 | 95% / 67% / 1.57 / $1,956. Variant I alone (report 162): 79% / 51% / 1.78 / $2,695 | 95% / 85% / 1.84 / $2,094. Gates still add about +3 pass, +9 paid points over the no-gate deploy. Not audited: R1/RULES/ON generators line by line; stop slippage on core legs.

xs/report_192_stress_three.py, log xs/report_192.log. 10-day block bootstrap (3,000), extra slippage 0-3 pts/trade, sign-flip placebo (5,000), 20% random missed trades, leave-one-year-out. Sizes as deploy (core u*sz, FIG-V 0.5 NQ, S1 0.2 NQ), no account rules.
Core: 2,522 trades, +8.2 pts avg, $161k, max DD $6.3k; P(profit) 100%, 5th pct $124k; +2 pts slippage still $113k; placebo p<0.0001; worst leave-one-year-out $119k.
FIG-V (1m): 635 trades, +1.12 avg, $7.1k, max DD $4.3k; P(profit) 93%, 5th pct -$0.6k; breaks even at 1.2 pts extra slippage; placebo p=0.054; 2020 contributes $4.3k and 2024 $4.1k of $7.1k.
S1 regime-on: 193 trades, +29.8 avg, $23.0k, max DD $2.6k; P(profit) 99.9%, 5th pct $9.8k; slippage-insensitive (wide stops); placebo p=0.0018; 2025 alone $15.1k of $23.0k, ex-2025 $8.0k.

## Report 195: checklist gaps on all 3 systems (entry-delay test vetoed by Rey: late entries sit at the peak)
- Loser slippage sweep (+0.25/.5/1/2 pts on every losing trade): all legs keep sign; test-era t at +1pt: RA 2.5, M1 2.2, RULES 3.0, V15a 3.3, R1 1.3. Combo test-era $ 136k -> 131k at +1, 126k at +2.
- Remove best 1/5/10% trades (per contract): M1 and V15a go negative at best-5/10%; RA, RULES stay positive; R1 negative at best-10%. Random-10% removal changes nothing. Edge is tail-concentrated (trend/breakout profile).
- Long/short: RA train long 9.5 (t1.3) short 8.8 (t1.5); test long 3.9 (t.6) short 23.3 (t3.2). V15a train long 9.9 (t3.2) short 0.3 (t.1); test long 22.1 (t2.3) short 35.4 (t2.4). Short side only works in test era for V15a.
- Per-year: RA/V15a/M1 2024 weak (RA -1.3, M1 1.8 pts), 2026 YTD negative on all small n; V15a bp-of-price edge not decaying (2022 40bp, 2025 6bp is down). M1 edge in bp falling from 18.5 (2018) to 3.4 (2025).
- R1 train era mean 0.14 pts t0.1: only works 2022+. ON+/ON- are not in prep() so not covered here.
- Combo: worst day -$2.2k, worst month -$2.5k at base unscaled sizes (before Lucid scaling).
- Large-cap: t 4.5, excess vs SPY 6.4bp/day t2.5; dropping best 5% of days takes mean to -2.6bp but SPY goes to -6.5bp (same tail dependence); 2022 +0.2% vs SPY -18%, 2025 14.8% vs 17.7%.
- Mid/small: t 3.0 absolute but excess vs equal-weight only t1.1 (0.3%/mo); best 20% months removed -> negative.
- Multiple testing: 373 report scripts in repo; Bonferroni |t| bar 3.8 at N=300. Only RULES 3.3, V15a 3.3 near it; RA 2.6, M1 2.4 do not clear a deflated bar.

330 clean 2026 flips; model = ATRP + EXT train-ECDF rank-sum (report 118 minus turnover), train 80th pct cut = 1.54 (it selects 159 = 48% of 2026 flips, so it is not a top-20% filter this year). ## Reports 201-202: pattern among big 2026 sub-$5 flips (xs/report_201_big_flip_pattern.py, report_202_pattern_check.py)
2026 (330 clean flips; 20 >=+50%, 8 >=+100%): big flips vs rest AUC: BASE (60d range/price) .66 p.015, PRIOR_DD (130d drawdown) .65 p.021, EXT .64 p.040, D52 .61; ATRP .56, volume spike (VX) .50, price .50. Seven of 8 doublers had R60 < 0 and prior drawdown 54-78%: bases, not breakouts. TIMING: April 2026 entries = 51 flips, mean +13.0%, 7 big, 5 of the 8 doublers (entered Apr 9-22); Jul-Nov flips mean -1% to -10%. 
Checked on 2019-Feb 2026 sub-$5 flips (1,863 with features; train 510 | test 1,353): AUC for BIG (>=+50%) PRIOR_DD .70 | .63, BASE .73 | .65 (same sign, PASS on AUC); P(BIG) bottom->top quintile 2-3% -> 13-35%. But median of top quintile is -3.6% | -10.6%, win 43% | 25%, ex-best-3 mean falls 70->47 | 15->5; both features correlate +0.8-0.9 with the ATRP+EXT outlier score. Within the high-OS2 half BASE still adds (test top-quintile mean +26.2% vs +8.1%, P(BIG) 16.2% vs 12.2%; train 79% vs 49%) = lead, not a selector. Volume spike at the flip does not separate.

## Reports 203-204: selectors for BIG sub-$5 flips (xs/report_203_selectors.py, report_204_critical_slowing.py)
Universe: 2,134 flips (outlier_features px<5: train 510 | test 1,353; plus 271 late-2026 flips from report 199). Cuts frozen on train. 15 selectors tried (Bonferroni |t|>=2.9). Earnings-based z-score untestable: earnings files cover 0% of sub-$5 names; quarterly self-history z (vs own 63/126/189/252d-ago values) used instead.
AUC for BIG (>=+50%) train | test | late-2026: BASE .733|.647|.629; PRIOR_DD .699|.631|.617; OS2 (ATRP+EXT) .739|.674|.535; P1 (ATRP*sqrt(volume ratio), physics pressure) .705|.686|.472; ZBASE .639|.535|.584; ZATR .619|.531|.448; COIL .443|.580|.468; DLOW .524|.493|.450; flip-day volume ratio .534|.590|.471; index drawdown .417|.503|.412. Quarterly-self-history z-scores fade in test: no.
Selected-subset test means (all flips +7.3%, ex-best3 +5.2%, median -6.9%): OS2 +25.1% (x3 +10.0%), BASE +28.2% (+8.0%), P1 +22.0% (+11.1%), OS2&BASE +59.8% (+16.2%, n60), BASE&VX +51.7% (+6.7%, n59), BASE&GATE +9.8%. Medians -10% to -13%, win 19-35%. Late-2026 (n271, 17 BIG): every selector near zero or negative (OS2 -12.9%, P1 -9.0%, BASE +10.4% / x3 -8.0%). Market-drawdown gate n<5 in late 2026: it does not explain April 2026 (index never reached -10%).
Report 204 (critical slowing down, AR1/variance rise/skew before the flip): AUC .506|.513|.546, .478|.551|.448, .506|.603|.582. No.
Verdict: BASE/PRIOR_DD and P1 are leads (same-sign AUC .63-.73 in train and test); none beats the all-flips book on the late-2026 sample, medians stay negative. Not adopted.

## Report 205: supply of big moves and catalyst theory (xs/report_205_supply_catalyst.py)
(A) Doubler supply (sub-$5 names at month start whose max close over the next 60 trading days >= 2x), per 100 names: 2019 4.4, 2020 24.1, 2021 5.0, 2022 4.7, 2023 9.8, 2024 10.7, 2025 11.8, 2026 5.1. 2025: Jan-Apr 6, May-Sep 15-21, Oct-Dec 7-9. 2026: Jan 4.6, Feb 2.8, Mar 6.1, Apr 15.1, May 6.3, Jun 2.2, Jul 0.0, Aug 2.6 (Sep-Nov not measurable yet: needs a 60d forward window). Vector flips whose peak reached +100%: Apr 7, May 3, Jun 1, Jul-Nov 0. So the late-2026 drought is a supply drought in this universe, not only a selection failure. ONFO and AKAN are not in any of our data/universes (neither is checkable); APP and CVNA are in the large-cap 15m cache but not in the sub-$5 universe.
(B) Catalyst theory (4 tests, sign declared): H1 8-K in prior 7d: BIG rate with vs without 21.6% vs 12.8% (train, ratio 1.69), 12.0% vs 8.0% (test, 1.50), 4.6% vs 6.8% (late 2026, 0.68). H2 insider open-market buy prior 30d: test 16.2% vs 8.7% (n37, 1.87); data ends 2026-05-06, late 2026 n=3. H3 13D prior 30d: 1.23 | 0.95 | 0.88 (nothing). H4 offering prior 14d: forms file starts 2024-10, n=7 test: untestable. No catalyst passes a multiple-testing bar; H1 is a weak same-sign lead through 2025.
(C) Supply persistence: lag-1 autocorrelation 0.68 is inflated by overlapping 60d windows; non-overlapping version (trailing 3 completed months vs current month) corr 0.15, Spearman 0.28 (n87). Weak.

Verdict: no options-ratio-like universe variable found for sub-$5; the options ratio works on large caps as a threshold (informed-flow universe) and small caps mostly have no listed options.

## Report 208: confluence count for BIG sub-$5 flips (xs/report_208_confluence.py)
5 equal-weight binary factors (BASE, PRIOR_DD, P1 physics pressure, name 252d ATRP each >= train 80th pct; 8-K in prior 7d); factors picked after seeing reports 201-207 so test is not clean; F1-F4 rank-correlated .64-.79. P(BIG >=+50%) by count 0 | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4: train 5.2 | 18.5 | 26.8 | 17.5 | 41.2%; test 4.9 | 11.8 | 11.4 | 16.0 | 17.6 (| 21.4% at 5, n14); Spearman(count, BIG) +0.30 | +0.15 | +0.06 (late 2026). Returns do not follow: Spearman(count, r) -0.03 | -0.20 | -0.15; test median falls -5.5% -> -13%, ex-best3 mean 1.8% (C=0) -> -1.3% (C=4) -> -18.9% (C=5+); late 2026 C>=3 n19: BIG 0%, mean -16.1%. 1H flips Aug-Nov 2026 (n1,343): count vs net r Spearman -0.29 (C=0 mean -1.31%, C=4 -6.71%, C=5+ -9.73%), >=+10% share flat 6-9%. Verdict: confluence raises the chance of a tail, not the expected return; the same names carry bigger losses. Not adopted.

Started: xs/fetch_1h_lowpx.py (Yahoo 1H bars back to Dec 2023 for every name that closed < $5 on 20+ days since Dec 2023) for a multi-regime 1H flip test.

## Reports 210-212: 1H vs daily flips over three regimes, hybrid, probe-rate regime sensor (xs/report_210_1h_vs_daily.py, report_211_hybrid_1h_entry_daily_hold.py, report_212_probe_regime.py)
Data: Yahoo 1H bars 2023-12-18..2026-11-13 (unadjusted) for 317 names that closed < $5 on 20+ days since Dec 2023 (alive today = survivorship), 299 usable; flips entry >= 2024-02, px < $5, split trades dropped; net of 100bp; H1 = 2024-02..2025-06, H2 = 2025-07..2026-11. No slot limits; sums of trade returns are not a portfolio.
210 per flip (H1 | H2): daily n559 | 537, mean +17.9% | +5.2%, median -7.9% | -8.1%, win 28 | 25%, >=+100% 5.9 | 5.2%, sum 9,994% | 2,791%, hold 33 | 28d. 1H n3,653 | 3,636, mean +1.35% | +0.12%, median -3.7%, >=+100% 0.8 | 0.6%, sum 4,942% | 440%, hold 5d.
210 doubling episodes (close doubles within 60d from a 10d-low < $5; 190 | 125): a flip inside the episode: daily 61% | 45%, 1H 91% | 86%; share of the move gone at first entry: daily 26% | 22%, 1H 7% | 8%. Rey's claim (1H catches the big ones earlier) holds in both halves; but first-trade realized >= +50% as share of all episodes: daily 21.1% | 15.2%, 1H 4.7% | 5.6% (1H exits in days).
211 HYBRID (1H entry; if the daily vector is bull at a daily close the trade is confirmed and exits on the daily bear flip; a 1H bear flip before confirmation = failed probe): n2,485 | 2,376, mean +6.44% | +0.67%, sum 15,995% | 1,582% (daily 9,994% | 2,791%), >=+100% 2.3 | 1.4%. Failed probes 76%: mean -3.66% | -4.34% (sum -6,943% | -7,890%); confirmed n586 | 559: mean +39.1% | +16.9%, median +2.3% | +1.0%, win 57 | 54%, >=+100% 9.6 | 5.9%. Episode capture first trade >= +50%: 13.2% | 10.4% (between 1H and daily). H_HYBRID FAILS the declared bar (must beat daily in both halves; loses H2). The early-entry premium exceeds the probe cost in H1 only.
212 trailing 10-day probe confirmation rate as regime sensor: top-minus-bottom tercile day-clustered t: hybrid -0.2 (H1) | +1.7 (H2); daily flips -1.1 | +2.3. Sign flips: FAIL (the 2025 burst started while the rate was at its low, 0.11 in Apr 2025).

## Report 213: capturable-move label, price-response asymmetry, impulse retention (xs/report_213_capturable_responsiveness.py; tests taken from an outside critique Rey attached)
Labels from the entry on daily highs/lows, 40 days, stop wins ties: L30 (+30% before -15%) base rate 27.5% train | 24.3% test | 24.8% late 2026 (steady, unlike BIG 14.3 | 8.9 | 6.3%); LATR (+3 ATR before -1.5 ATR) 39.4 | 35.6 | 32.3%.
Existing selectors on the volatility-scaled label: OS2 AUC .550 | .479 | .471, ATRP .550 | .479 | .500, BASE .525 | .485 | .517 = nothing. On L30 they keep .64-.68 | .61-.64. So the selectors pick volatile names (bigger absolute moves), not better trades per unit of risk.
ASY (log up-day return per dollar / down-day return per dollar, last 20d; OHLCV proxy): raw AUC L30 .505 | .519 | .550, LATR .530 | .525 | .529; matched on ATRP x R20 cells: L30 26.2% vs 22.6% test (z +1.6; train z +0.8), LATR 37.5% vs 33.8% (z +1.4; train +1.0); test AUC over controls +0.003 (L30), +0.012 (LATR). Same sign in every cell but below the bar (|z| >= 2, Bonferroni 2.64): weak lead. dASY (change vs own history): z +0.7 / +0.3, no. RETN (impulse retention): sign flips, no. Failed-bearish-pressure family = reports 123/124 (failed), not re-run.
