ORDER FLOW (MNQ 1-min signed volume, first candle): only 214 trades 2024-12..2025-11, second half n=33 -> aligned-minus-against +0.213R (t+0.5) then -0.435R (t-0.6): inconclusive, cannot meet the strict split. OPTIONS (QQQ net vanna/charm, NQ gex-wall ratio, prior day, to 2025-12): vanna terciles no pattern (low +0.154/+0.205 vs high +0.118/+0.207); charm-aligned WRONG sign train (+0.107 vs +0.180) then right in test (+0.247 vs +0.193); gex ratio unstable (test cell n 54). READ: of 9 conditioning tests only the gap alignment holds the same sign in both halves with a mechanism (overnight order imbalance continues into the open; Gao et al. intraday momentum; matches the 'gap rule' in the shown profile); it is a candidate to PRE-REGISTER (gap-aligned, large-gap), not yet admitted (difference t only 1.3-1.4 per half, second-stage cut). Volatility/ATRP/VIX/order-flow/options-flow add nothing on this engine. Not yet through the Lucid payout harness.

## Report 337 (Nov 18 2026): FORMAL STRESS TEST 1/3 - NQ prop algo (8 models RA, M1, R1, ON+, ON-, RULES, V15a, S1; NZ/CH/FIG-V excluded; 1 contract per model from the leg tables, before prop-account rules, 2018-01 -> 2026-11). 6,263 trades, 4,433 pts/yr (3,145 incl. the sparse 2014-17 legs, dropped), +6.28 pts/trade, ~706 trades/yr. S1 Sharpe 1.85 (daily pts), PSR(0) 1.000; Deflated Sharpe (assumed skill-less trial SR sd 0.5 annualised; real trial count unlogged): N=50 0.996, N=200 0.960, N=1,000 0.799, N=5,000 0.515 (expected max skill-less SR 1.14/1.38/1.63/1.84). S2 stationary block bootstrap: P(SR<0) 0.0000, SR 5-95% 1.48-2.25, pts/yr 5-95% 3,247-5,699, max DD actual -1,945 pts vs bootstrap median -1,873 and worst-5% -2,766. S3 sign-flip permutation p=0.0000 (trades and days). S4 extra cost per trade (pts): +0.5 4,080 pts/yr SR 1.71 (9/9 yrs +); +1 3,727 SR 1.56 (8/9); +2 3,021 SR 1.27 (8/9); +4 1,609 SR 0.68 (7/9). S5 concentration: removing the top 1% of trades leaves 68% of profit, top 2% leaves -7% (i.e. the best 2% of trades carry ~107% of total P&L), top 5% leaves -87%; by days: top 1% leaves 47%, top 2% 28%, top 5% -28%. By year pts: 2018 2,876; 2019 519; 2020 3,607; 2021 4,440; 2022 5,970; 2023 2,885; 2024 5,164; 2025 11,398 (2.6x the mean); 2026 YTD 2,469; leave-one-year-out pts/yr 3,549 (drop 2025) to 4,931 (drop 2019). Per model pts/trade: RA 10.5, M1 7.3, R1 7.7, ON+ 5.8, ON- 1.8, RULES 2.2, V15a 12.2, S1 28.0. S6 halves: 2018-22 SR 1.90 (3,489 pts/yr) | 2023-26 SR 1.91 (5,661 pts/yr). READ: the edge is statistically real (permutation, bootstrap, DSR >0.5 even at 5,000 trials) and survives +2 pts/trade; the weaknesses are (1) extreme tail concentration (a trend-day engine: the best 2% of trades ARE the profit), (2) ON-/RULES earn under 2.2 pts/trade and fall first under cost, (3) 2025 is a third of all profit, (4) not tested here: look-ahead replacement, parameter neighbourhood/CPCV-PBO (no variant matrix stored), late entry (no intrabar paths in leg tables).

## Report 338 (Nov 18 2026): FORMAL STRESS TEST 2/3 - large-cap options-ratio vector screener (6 slots, 8% breaker, 54 research names, 4,253 trades taken, 2019-01 -> 2026-09, simulator of 303/324; baseline CAGR 23.6%, max DD -23.5% over the full span (-13.0/-16.2 within the two sub-periods), Sharpe 2.60). S1 PSR(0) 1.000; Deflated Sharpe N=50 1.000, 200 0.999, 1,000 0.992, 5,000 0.970 (skill-less trial SR sd 0.5 assumed). S2 block bootstrap: P(SR<0) 0.0003, SR 5-95% 1.31-3.91, CAGR 5-95% 11.5-36.5%, max DD actual -23.5% vs bootstrap median -21.6%, worst-5% -34.4%. S3 random-entry placebo (same tickers, same hold in bars, 2,000 draws): actual mean trade +0.316% vs placebo +0.194% (sd 0.057%), p=0.015 -> the flip beats random timing in the same names but over half of the mean trade is the names' own drift. S4 extra cost per trade (bp, on top of 2+2): +5 CAGR 18.1% (Sharpe 2.02), +10 12.8% (1.45), +20 2.9% (0.37), +40 -14.3% (-1.61). S5 LATE ENTRY (price = that 15m bar's open, exit unchanged): delay 15 min CAGR 12.4% (Sharpe 1.45, mean candidate trade +0.196% vs +0.276%); 30 min 8.7% (DD -32.6%, Sharpe 1.07); 60 min 1.8% (Sharpe 0.27). S6 neighbourhood slots {4,5,6,8,10} x breaker {0,5,8,12%}: 20/20 profitable, CAGR min 16.8% median 20.8%, Sharpe min 1.99 (fewer slots = higher CAGR: 4 slots 31-36% except the 5% breaker 18%). S7 concentration: removing the best 5% of taken trades (212) -> CAGR -14.1%, DD -69.6%; best 10% -> -27.7%; by year 2019 21.7%, 2020 61.4%, 2021 21.8%, 2022 -14.7%, 2023 49.7%, 2024 39.2%, 2025 10.2%, 2026 9.5%; halves 2019-22 CAGR 19.6% (Sharpe 2.22) | 2023-26 28.1% (3.00). READ: statistically real and not a parameter accident, but (1) EXTREMELY execution-sensitive: a 15-minute late entry halves the CAGR (the flip is a breakout; the first bars carry the edge), 30 minutes leaves 9%; backtest enters the next bar open, so an alert-then-manual-entry workflow must be measured; (2) cost-sensitive: +10 bp costs half the CAGR; (3) heavy-tail dependence (best 5% of trades carry all the profit, like the NQ stack); (4) 2022 was negative, 2025-26 are weak (10%). Not tested: look-ahead replacement (needs live signal code).

## Report 339-340 (Nov 18 2026): late flip entries only on a pullback (Rey's rule), and does the pullback HOLD?
- 339: delay k bars, enter only if open_k <= flip entry open. 15 min: 43% of flips pull back; those same trades return -0.10% from the original entry vs +0.27% from the late entry. Book of pullback-only 15-min entries: CAGR 19.1%, DD -23.5%, Sharpe 2.51 (blind 15-min delay 12.4%; baseline 23.6%/-23.5%/2.60). Pullback >= 0.25%: 12.7%, DD -15.4%, Sharpe 2.03. 30 min: 17.9% / DD -16.6%; 60 min: 14.3% / DD -15.1%. Chased flips (never pull back) are the strong ones (+0.77% to +1.08% from the original entry) and are skipped.
- 340 (Rey: a pullback may be a failed break; volume must hold after the open): pullbacks split by structure held (low stays >= flip bar low) and volume held (>= 0.7x flip bar volume). Late-entry return is ~+0.25-0.3% in every cell; held vs failed structure does not separate in test (+0.24% vs +0.29% at 15 min); volume FADED is not worse than volume held (test +0.32% vs +0.22%), the opposite of the hypothesis. Books on held-only entries are unstable (15 min: 12.5%, DD -30.5%; 30 min: 14.5%, DD -11.2%). Conclusion: these two cuts do not tell a retest from a failed break; no admitted filter. Crude proxies (one volume cut, bar-low structure); a better test needs a rule fixed in advance.

## Report 341-342 (Nov 22 2026): AMD Po3 (compression -> sweep -> return) on NQ, and the consolidation-vs-slow-trend measure on vector flips
- 341: Po3 ported (absolute boundaries, defaults; entry next-bar open, stop sweep extreme + 0.4 ATR, target fib 1.5, 64-bar timeout, 0.75 pt cost). 24h feed: 4,932 trades, R -0.02 test / +0.02 train, pts/trade +0.76 train | +0.05 test; mostly overnight because the width percentile is ranked against a quiet-overnight history (midday slots empty). RTH-only: 1,574 trades, -0.90 | -0.44 pts, R -0.08 | -0.03, long/short flip sign between halves. Baseline = no edge.
- Rey's critique (slow trend looks like compression) holds directionally in RTH: efficiency ratio ER of the accumulation window, high tercile loses -5.7 pts (t-3.0) train and -5.6 (t-2.4) test; DRIFT high tercile -3.9/-5.4. Low/mid terciles are only +0.3 to +3.8 pts (t<1.3), so removing slow-trend setups gets to roughly +1.5 to +2 pts/trade on ~100 trades/yr, not an edge. 24h run: high ER sign not consistent (+0.35 train, -1.31 test). Not admitted as a trading rule; admitted as a FILTER fact (fading a sweep in a smooth drift loses).
- 342: same measure (ER20, Donchian-20 compression percentile) on the 12,320 large-cap vector flips: no gradient (train ER low/mid/high +0.48/+0.32/+0.59%, test +0.25/+0.16/+0.25%); compression gradient flat in train, weak in test (+0.26/+0.28/+0.13%). Book drops: high-ER third 16.7%, low-ER third 20.0%, compressed third 18.6% vs baseline 23.6%. The vector's flips do not depend on prior consolidation.
- Quarterly Theory indicator (time-cycle labeller): 90-min session slot breakdown of Po3 entries is sign-unstable across halves (slot 12: -6.9 | +1.5; slot 13: +1.5 | -3.8); no admitted cycle effect. Not tested on the vector yet.

## Report 343 (Nov 22 2026): Z-score (time-of-day-adjusted EMA residual) and jump-diffusion split (Lee-Mykland J, bipower variation, jump share RJ) on vector flips; aligned-gap Z on NQ ORB
- Vector flips (12,318 with features; baseline +0.46% train | +0.22% test per trade): aligned Z terciles train +0.35/+0.58/+0.45%, test +0.21/+0.27/+0.19% (middle best in both, no monotonic gradient). Flip-bar jump stat J: train low/mid/high +0.40/+0.45/+0.53%, test +0.25/+0.30/+0.15% (sign flips between halves). Jump-driven flips (J>=3, 33% of flips): +0.68% train vs +0.16% test; diffusive (|J|<1.5): +0.40 | +0.25. Jump share of the prior 32 bars: high third best in both (+0.64 | +0.33) but the middle third is worst, not monotonic. Book: drop top-J third 24.3% / DD -16.5% / Sharpe 2.82; drop high-Z third 24.1% / -20.6% / 2.91; drop low-Z third 19.3%; drop J>=3 21.0% (baseline 23.6% / -23.5% / 2.60). The book gains come from the test half while per-trade train points the other way: not admitted; at most a drawdown-trimming candidate to pre-register and re-test on forward flips.
- NQ ORB, continuous gap Z (aligned gap in ADR): top third +12.2 | +12.9 pts per trade (t+2.4 | +2.6), low and mid thirds ~0 to +4; same-sign both halves, confirms the gap-alignment survivor from report 334, adds nothing new.

## Report 344 (Nov 22 2026): statistical properties of the NQ stack (shape, clustering, day-before predictability, Gaussian auction test)
- A shape: stack trades (n 6,263) skew +4.6, excess kurtosis 63; Hill tail index of winners ~2.1 (S1 1.9, ON- 2.7, V15a 2.75, M1 3.0; losers 3-5): near-infinite variance, so sd/Sharpe summaries are fragile; Student-t df ~2.6 beats Gaussian by >1,500 log-lik. Profit from the top 5% of trades exceeds 100% (M1 186%, ON- 648%) because the rest net negative. RA's tail row is degenerate (near-binary payoffs), ignore.
- B clustering: |daily stack P&L| autocorrelates (lag1 +0.28, lag5 +0.16, lag20 +0.10, both halves, Ljung-Box p<0.0001); signed P&L ~0 (lag1 -0.02). Big days cluster, direction does not.
- C next-day stack P&L by trailing-5-day sum |P&L| (cuts 175/335): low/mid/high train +7.4/+4.8/+21.0 pts (t+2.3 high), test +8.8/+20.0/+24.0 (t+3.4 high); same sign both halves, mechanism = trend-day clustering. Candidate for sizing (not a gate: the low bucket is still positive). Other features not admitted: trailing 5d stack P&L (train low best +19.0, test low +25.6, high +2.8 | +20.4, non-monotonic), NQ jump count (flat), jump share RJ (train mid best, test high best +36.6). 4 features x 3 terciles tested: multiple-testing caution; to be pre-registered as one sizing rule and re-tested.
- D daily NQ RTH z=(close-open)/ADR14: sd 0.71, skew -0.58, excess kurtosis +2.2; tail mass beyond |z|>2 is 1.4% vs 0.5% Gaussian; 2-component mixture beats Gaussian (BIC 4952 vs 5092): balanced component 65% (mean +0.18, sd 0.5) and a wide component 35% (mean -0.28, sd 0.93); wide days skew down.

## Report 345 (Nov 22 2026): rotate 15m and 1h vector exits by context (Rey: closing early has always hurt)
- 1h vector (same machine, RTH 15m aggregated to 09:30-based hourly bins), exit = first 1h bear flip after the 15m entry; context = 1h regime at the last completed 1h bar (bull at entry for 34% of flips). Sanity: recomputed 15m returns match b302 (corr 1.0).
- Per trade the 1h exit is much bigger but holds ~18 days vs ~3 (train +1.90% | test +0.94% vs +0.46% | +0.22%), so compare the book: 6 slots A (all 1h) 16.5% CAGR / DD -23.3% / Sharpe 3.33; B (1h exit only when 1h-bull) 23.9% / -28.6% / 2.67; C (later-of when 1h-bull) 25.1% / -28.9% / 2.80; live 15m exit 23.6% / -23.5% / 2.60. Slot sensitivity N=12: live 17.7% / -16.5% / 2.44, A 19.2% / -24.1% / 4.26, B 22.7% / -23.1% / 3.09, C 22.4% / -23.4% / 3.05. At equal drawdown (live N=6 vs B N=12: 23.6% / -23.5% vs 22.7% / -23.1%) CAGR is the same and Sharpe is higher (2.60 vs 3.09): the longer exit mostly keeps capital invested rather than adding edge; holding costs deeper drawdowns. Not admitted as better; B at N=12 is an equal-risk alternative to re-test on forward flips. Early 15m exits did not hurt more than late ones inside the 1h-not-bull group (1h exits there look best per trade but hold ~20 days).
- Forward log: xs/forward_flip_log.py (appends raw flips of the 54-name research set with features, age and pullback flags to data/forward_log.parquet; `score` fills exits). The live options-ratio gate is not applied there.
- Slot occupancy (Rey: holding slots makes us miss flips): 12,320 candidates; 6 slots take 4,253 (35%), 8 take 45%, 12 take 63%. Mean return taken vs skipped: +0.316% vs +0.255% (6 slots), +0.275% vs +0.278% (12 slots): the ranker adds little, the book is capacity-limited, so any exit that holds longer costs entries. A rotation rule (replace the stalest/weakest open position with a fresh high-rank flip) is untested.
- Forward log first run (Nov 22, last bar Nov 20 15:45): 38 raw flips of the 54 names over Nov 18-20; almost all failed in the Nov 19-20 high-beta selloff (MU -14% on Nov 20 alone, GS -6.7%); 31 of 38 already bear-flipped (7 still bull). Raw flips, no options-ratio gate.

## Report 346-347 (Nov 22 2026): shorting under the vector, and the QQQ weekly/daily regime as a gate on the long flips (Rey: weekly vector bear, looks like .com)
- State: QQQ weekly bear flip on the week closing Nov 20 (close 608.45, high 762.86); daily bear since the Nov 2 flip (entry 695.32, +12.5% for a short so far, adverse excursion +0.9%).
- 346 weekly bear regimes 1999-2026 (entry next open to bull-flip open, short P&L after 2bp each side): 2000-04 to 2003-06 +61.4% (held 1,141 days, squeeze +33%); every other regime LOST: 2004 -10.9%, 2006 -6.2%, 2008-03 -7.6%, 2008-10 to 2009-11 -10.4% (flip came after the crash), 2011 -9.3%, 2020-03 -17.1%, 2022-02 to 2023-06 -5.2%, 2026-04 -14.3% (the March/April whipsaw). QQQ weekly vector short CAGR -2.2% (1999-2026), -5.5% (2019-26); daily vector short -5.5% / -9.1%; long+short worse than long-only in both (daily both 3.4% vs long 9.4%; 2019-26 2.4% vs 12.7%). Daily bear regimes: 77, win 16%, mean -0.75%, median -2.9%.
- Single names (54 large caps, 2018-26): 1,633 daily-vector bear regimes, mean short -2.77% (t-10.2, win 19%); train -2.52%, test -2.99%, 2018 -2.41%; negative every year. Caveat: the 54 are today's large-cap survivors (biased against shorts); the QQQ result is not.
- 347 gate: long flips taken while the QQQ WEEKLY regime is bear do BETTER, not worse (test +0.43% vs +0.17% per trade; train +1.36% on only 57 flips); daily bear also better (+0.32% vs +0.19% test, +0.79% vs +0.39% train). Book skipping weekly-bear flips 16.4% CAGR (Sharpe 2.20), skipping daily-bear 16.4% / DD -18.2%, skipping either 11.9%, vs 23.6% / -23.5% / 2.60. Mechanism candidate: the best long flips are the rebound bounces inside bear regimes. Not a gate.
- Conclusion: the vector confirms a bear late (it lags the break), so a short under it gave back the gains except in the 3-year dot-com decline. Not admitted as a short system; long flips in a bear tape are not worse.

## Report 348 (Nov 22 2026): follow-through protection stops on the long flips (Rey: protect instead of classify; faster stop on flips that don't follow through; frees slots)
- 8 pre-declared rules on the 15m path of all 12,320 flips (6-slot book, 2+2bp): live vector 23.6% CAGR / DD -23.5% / Sharpe 2.60 (4,253 trades). Breakeven after +0.5% / +1% / +2%: 13.1% / 13.8% / 14.1% CAGR, DD -16.9% / -18.6% / -22.2%, Sharpe 1.76 / 1.70 / 1.68 (7,337 / 5,865 / 4,936 trades taken). Time stops (4 bars flat; 8 bars < +0.25%; 16 bars < +0.5%): 18.1% / 16.8% / 16.5%, DD -20.0% / -22.4% / -18.8%, Sharpe 2.14 / 1.96 / 1.88. Tightened stop only when no follow-through (8,.25%,.3%) 13.6% / DD -25.3%; (16,.5%,.5%) 20.7% / DD -19.6% / Sharpe 2.29 (best). Per trade every rule cut the mean (test +0.07% to +0.16% vs live +0.22%) and win rate fell for BE (14-25%).
- Even with the freed slots (up to 7,337 trades taken vs 4,253) the book lost CAGR in every variant: the vector's wide stop is what lets the heavy-tail winners run (report 344: tail index ~2.1). Protection trades 3 to 10 points of CAGR for 3 to 7 points of drawdown, no variant dominates the live exit. Not admitted. Untested: scale-out (partial at +x, trail the rest) and slot rotation (swap the stalest/weakest open flip for a fresh high-rank flip).

## Report 349 (Nov 22 2026): slot swap and scale-out on the long flips (6 slots, 8% breaker, 2+2bp)
- Live: 23.6% CAGR / DD -23.5% / Sharpe 2.60 (4,253 trades; 2019-21 33.8% Sh 4.15, 2022-26 17.5% Sh 1.85).
- SWAP S1 (replace lowest unrealized if < 0): 21.1% / -17.2% / 2.26, 1,563 swaps, victims -0.53% vs swapped-in flips +0.19%; 2019-21 38.3% Sh 4.52 but 2022-26 11.3% Sh 1.19 (halves disagree). S2 (replace oldest, held >= 2 sessions and < +0.5%): 25.2% / -21.1% / 2.72, only 73 swaps, 2019-21 33.8% (same as live), 2022-26 20.2% Sh 2.05; same sign both halves but 73 swaps is too few to separate from noise. S3 (replace weakest whenever < +0.5%): 10.0% / -41.4% / 1.11, 5,770 swaps, 2022-26 -3.6%: over-rotation destroys the book.
- SCALE-OUT 50% at +1% / +2% / +4%: 12.8% / 15.8% / 18.3% CAGR, DD -15.5% / -14.6% / -21.6%, Sharpe 1.97 / 2.14 / 2.23; per-trade mean +0.15% / +0.18% / +0.22% vs live +0.28%. Banks follow-through but halves the tail: trades CAGR for drawdown, Sharpe lower than live. Not admitted.
- Net: only the S2 swap is non-negative in both halves (small, 73 events); watch on the forward log, not live-ready. Aggressive rotation and early profit-taking both cost more than they free.

## Report 350 (Nov 22 2026): do vector flips do better after the opening range exists / above it? (Rey: if ORB works, post-15/30-min entries should be higher probability)
- Timing: flips entering 09:30-09:45 (31% of all) train +0.33% | test +0.28% (win 31-32%); 09:45-10:00 +0.99% | +0.20%; 10:00-10:30 +0.58% | +0.05%; 10:30-12:00 +0.31% | +0.25%; 12:00-14:00 +0.19% | +0.27%. No time bucket is higher in both halves; the first bar is as good as any (the first-bar flips are open-gap carry-over).
- ORB state (last completed bar vs the session opening range): above OR15 train +0.39% | test +0.16%, inside +0.70% | +0.24%, below ~0 (n 37/86); OR30 above +0.25% | +0.17%, inside +0.52% | +0.25%. Flips with price already ABOVE the opening range are not better than flips inside it (same sign both halves, inside higher).
- Book (6 slots): baseline 23.6% / -23.5% / 2.60; skip first-bar flips 20.0% / -18.0% / 2.33; skip first 30 min 14.2% / -15.4% / 1.89; post-OR15 only if above 17.0% / -20.1% / 1.96; post-OR30 only if above 23.1% / -18.9% / 2.52; only flips above OR15 12.3% / -19.3% / 1.73. Win rate stays 27-38% in every cell.
- Conclusion: the NQ ORB edge (trend-day continuation, gap-aligned, report 333-334) does not transfer to the vector flip as a timing or position-vs-range filter; the flip is already its own breakout (pivot ratchet), and early flips carry real return.

## Report 351-353 (Nov 22 2026): literature-led candidates (turning-point states, short with its own trail, NQ intraday momentum + gamma, overnight, stocks-in-play ORB)
- 351A long flips by QQQ state (weekly slow x daily fast vector) at entry: BULL 71% of flips train +0.39% | test +0.16%; CORRECTION (wk bull, dy bear) +0.71% | +0.25%; BEAR (both bear) +1.36% (n57) | +0.38%; REBOUND (wk bear, dy bull) n/a | +0.51% (n720). Order BULL < CORRECTION < BEAR holds in both halves (rebound only testable in the later period); gradient real but skipping any state costs CAGR (skip BULL 13.8%, CORRECTION 20.0%, BEAR 21.1%, REBOUND 21.5% vs 23.6%). Use as a rank/swap context, not a filter.
- 351B QQQ short with its own trail (daily bars, exit at the first of vector bull flip or close > lowest-low-since-entry + k x ATR14): vector short -5.3% CAGR -> k=2 -1.0%, k=3 -0.9% (mean +0.26%, win 28%), k=4 -3.2%; long+short 8.4% / DD -33.3% (k=3) vs long-only 9.4% / -45.3%. With the weekly-bear state filter (BEAR state only, k=3): 12 trades, mean +8.2%, win 58%, short-only +2.9% CAGR / DD -24.6%, long+short 12.6% / -33.3%; episodes 2000-03 +87% (4 trades), 2008 +4.7% (1), 2022-23 +9.2% (6, win 33%). Too few trades to admit; the waterfall-trail mechanism (pivot ratchet has no pivot highs in a waterfall) is consistent with Rey's 2008 observation. Weekly bars k=3: 9 trades mean -8.0%. 54 large caps daily bear + trail k=3: -1.48% per regime (t-6.9) vs vector short -2.77% (survivor-biased set), still negative in every period.
- 352 NQ: last-30-min momentum signal (rest of day) -> last 30 min: train +0.67 | test -2.14 pts/day net (hit rate 50%); first-30-min variant -1.4 | -2.0; no effect. Net-gamma flag (prior-day QQQ (call-put GEX)/(call+put), data to 2025-12): negative-gamma days +0.52 | +1.35 pts, positive-gamma days -0.28 | -5.44 (t-3.0 test) but train does not agree; not admitted. Overnight (16:00 to 09:25, long, after 0.75 cost): +7.8 pts (t+2.3, win 59%) train | +4.3 (t+1.0) test, up-day-first +11.0 | +2.0, down-day-first +3.8 | +6.9: positive both halves but it is long-beta exposure in a bull market (not tested as separate from drift), buy-the-dip not confirmed; 02:00-03:00 hour +0.57 | +0.05 pts (NY Fed drift hour not reproduced).
- 353 stocks-in-play 5m ORB on the 34 large caps with 5m bars (54,548 name-days, 2018-26): stop 0.10 x ATR14 as in the paper: win 5-8%, -0.65R to -0.80R every cut (stop inside the mega-cap 5m noise); stop at the opposite end of the first candle: all name-days -0.05R, top-3 rv0 -0.01R | 0.00R (long +0.05R | +0.02R, short -0.08R | -0.03R), bottom third -0.06R | -0.07R: relative volume ranks monotonically in both halves but nothing is positive after costs. Not an edge on this universe (mega-caps are rarely in play).

- 354 (6 slots, QQQ weekly x daily state, 29% of candidate flips non-BULL): P1 priority within a bar = identical book (23.6%/-23.5%/2.60, order already dominated by crowding); P2 swap (non-BULL flip replaces the weakest open BULL slot, only 15 swaps) 25.5% / -23.5% / 2.80 (train 34.0 | test 20.4); P3 reserve (BULL flips <= 4 of 6 slots) 25.1% / -18.3% / 2.99 (train 30.4 vs 33.8 | test 21.9 vs 17.5, Sharpe up in both halves 4.35 vs 4.15, 2.41 vs 1.85) BUT neighbours: reserve 2 18.1%, 3 19.3%, 5 24.0% (Sharpe 2.75); placebo (random 29% label, same rule, 50 draws) Sharpe 2.56 mean, real 2.99 beats 92% of draws (8% match or exceed). Reading: no option-implied variable beyond the ratio's liquidity content separates flips; the one same-sign lead is Johnson-So abnormal O/S (bearish informed flow).

## Report 362 (Nov 22 2026): speculation / flow / VWAP (xs/report_362_speculation_flow.py; 38 option names, 6,506 flips with option features, VWAP/rvol on 4,807)
Nine declared hypotheses, terciles cut on train, week-clustered t, top-minus-bottom mean trade (train | test): H1 OTM call share +0.17% | -0.21%; H2 short-dated call share -0.12 | +0.13; H3 abnormal call volume -0.14 | +0.02; H4 abnormal total option volume +0.05 | +0.02 (same sign, t<0.3); H5 strike concentration -0.08 | -0.07; H6 OTM put share +0.01 | -0.05; H7 call/put ratio -0.47 (t -2.3) | +0.01 (wrong sign in train); H8 close vs VWAP +0.01 | +0.01 (t 0.0 | 0.1); H9 flip-bar relative volume -0.05 | -0.03. 0 of 9 pass; share of >=+5% flips differs by <2pts almost everywhere. Open interest / next-day OI change NOT tested: no OI history on disk (theta_compact has per-contract volume only); needs a ThetaData pull. Speculation/lottery demand, targeted strikes and VWAP location do not identify bigger flips beyond what the flip already carries.

## Report 363 (Nov 22 2026): open interest tests (xs/oi_compact.py on Rey's machine -> xs/ext/oi_daily/<TK>.csv, 35 names; the raw ThetaData files had kind=='oi' rows that theta_compact.py had dropped)
OI is published ~06:30 and reflects the prior close; feature for trade date T = report(T+1) - report(T) per contract, 0-60 DTE, used on the next trading day. Seven declared hypotheses, train | test top-minus-bottom third (week-clustered t): O1 call turnover -0.10 | -0.21; O2 call OI build +0.11 | -0.03; O3 put OI build -0.07 | +0.11; O4 call opening share +0.09 | -0.13; O5 OI call/put +0.14 | +0.06 (same sign, t 0.7/0.4); O6 net bullish build +0.08 | -0.07; O7 put turnover +0.36 (t 1.5) | +0.04. 0 of 7 pass; >=+5% share differs by under 3 pts. Insider/opening-flow proxies do not identify bigger flips.

## Report 364 (Nov 22 2026): ratio with open interest (xs/report_364_oi_ratio.py)
R_oi = (call+put OI x100)/prior-day share volume (median 10.9; Spearman 0.33 with live ratio), R_turn = option volume/OI (median 0.22). Tercile top-bottom (train | test): R_oi -0.20 (t -1.0) | -0.05; R_turn +0.27 (t 1.1) | +0.18 (t 1.2); live ratio +0.08 | +0.11. Book pick order on the live universe: LIVE 23.6% / Sharpe 2.60 (train 33.8 | test 17.5); R_oi high-first 17.3% (34.1 | 7.8); R_oi low-first 17.9% (30.5 | 10.7); R_turn high-first 19.5% (35.6 | 10.3); R_turn low-first 13.9%. OI does not improve the ratio; R_turn has the right sign in both halves but t only ~1.1-1.2 and loses as a book order. Not admitted.

## Report 365 (Nov 22 2026): Merton jump-diffusion (xs/report_365_jump_diffusion.py)
Pooled standardised daily returns of the 54 names: train diffusion sd 0.64, ~49 jumps/yr (lam 0.196/day), mean jump +0.01 sd, jump sd 1.69 sd, jump share of variance 57%; test diffusion 0.69, ~51 jumps/yr, mean -0.02 sd, jump sd 1.57, jump share 51%. Stable across eras, mean jump ~0: jumps are symmetric fat tails, not directional. Declared hypothesis (flips raise upside-jump arrival; up-jump = daily return > +3 trailing-60d sigmas): P(up-jump in next 5 / 10 days) after a flip vs the name's baseline: 4.4% vs 3.9% (t 0.8) | 3.6% vs 3.7% (t -0.1); 10d 7.5% vs 7.5% | 7.2% vs 7.2%. Down-jumps after flips: 5d -0.8 (t -1.0) | +0.7 (t 1.4); 10d -0.9 | +1.4 (t 1.9, wrong sign vs 'not higher' only in test). 0 of 2 up tests pass. The flip does not raise the rate of upside jumps; its edge is not a jump-arrival clock. NOT admitted.

## Report 366 (Nov 22 2026): context replay + volatility z-score (xs/report_366_context_replay_and_vol_z.py, 366b_oversold_skip.py)
A. Walk-forward replay (fit on flips exited before Jan 1 of year Y, predict year Y, 2021-26; 114 features): logistic P(strong = ret > +5%) pooled OOS AUC 0.576 (by year .56 .61 .52 .57 .63 .62); top decile P(strong) 10.8% vs 6.3% overall (1.7x) but the top decile mean trade is only +0.39% vs +0.24% (t 1.0) and top>all in 6/6 years; model overpredicts the top decile (17% predicted vs 10.8% realised). Analog 300-NN: AUC 0.572 but top decile mean +0.20% (t -0.3). P(win) AUC 0.547. Strict pass (t>=2 on the decile mean) NOT met; it ranks the tail probability modestly, not the mean.
B. Vol z-score, z = 5d return / (sigma x sqrt5) with sigma measured BEFORE the window. All stock-days: |z|>2 share train/test: reg 8.4/8.2%, YZ 6.8/6.7% (closest to normal 4.6%), HAR 11.1/11.9% (Parkinson-range sigma vs close-based move: scale mismatch). Oversold z<-2 does not reliably rebound: P(further 5d fall) 47-50%; mean 5d return -0.2 to -0.7% in 2019-21 (t -0.1 to -0.3) vs +0.5 to +0.6% in 2022-26 (t 1.4-1.8): era-dependent. At bull flips oversold flips are WORSE (opposite to declared direction): reg -0.65% (t -1.6) | -0.41% (t -3.1); YZ -0.97% (t -2.2) | -0.39% (t -2.5); HAR -0.67% (t -2.0) | -0.08% (t -0.5). Extended z>+2 flips too few in train (n 2-14). 
366b (post-hoc, 3 variants looked at) as a book SKIP rule: live 23.6% / Sharpe 2.60 (train 33.8 | test 17.5); skip z_reg<-2 26.1% / 2.84 (31.5 | 22.7); skip z_yz<-2 26.0% / 2.85 (28.8 | 24.3); skip z_har<-2 22.2%; placebo skip of 749 random flips p95 CAGR 27.0% (test 22.7%). Train falls vs live (33.8 to 28.8) while test rises, and the result sits inside the placebo range: NOT admitted. Candidate for the forward log as a shadow flag (YZ-oversold at the flip), mechanism: the flip is a bounce inside a downtrend.

## Report 367 (Nov 22 2026): last-week illustration of the YZ vol z-score (xs/last_week_fetch.py, xs/report_367_last_week_check.py)
Nov 16-20 2026, bull flips, outcome = next same-timeframe bear flip (else marked to Nov 20 15:45), 4bp cost. 11 names flipped on both 15m and 1h; random 5 (seed 7): MCD, UBER, PANW, WMT, UNH = 15 flips: 2 good (both PANW, +9.6% 15m / +4.3% 1h, marked, z_yz -0.59 = not oversold), 13 bad. Only 1 flip was flagged oversold (UNH 15m, z_yz -4.6; -0.1% marked), so the flag did not separate good from bad in the sample. All 53 15m flips of the week: mean -1.5% net, ~10% green (a down week; trades stopped out fast); oversold n=3 mean -2.1% vs -1.5%; Spearman(z_yz, ret) +0.39 (lower z = worse, direction of report 366) but one market episode = few independent observations. 1h: 12 flips, 1 green. Illustration only, not a test.

Declared higher -> BETTER. EMA50>EMA200 (28% of flips) vs not: +0.18% (t 0.9) | +0.03% (t 0.3). d200 top vs bottom third: +0.35% (t 1.6) | -0.04% (t -0.4). d50 top vs bottom third +0.51% (t 2.6) | +0.09% (t 0.8) (win 38/28%, 34/28%). ## Report 370 (Nov 22 2026): EMA spans x timeframes, EMA z-score, Bollinger (xs/report_370_ema_bollinger_grid.py)
36 flip-bar features (DIST and ZEMA for EMA 20/50/100/200 on 15m/1h/daily, Bollinger %B and bandwidth, EMA stack flags), last completed bar per timeframe, two-sided, train 2019-21 | test 2022-26, tercile top-minus-bottom mean trade, week-clustered t. SOLID (|t|>=2 both halves, same sign): 0. WEAK: 1 (15m dist50: +0.43% t 2.2 | +0.05% t 0.4). Pattern: 15m extension above the EMAs paid in train (zema20 +0.50% t 2.6, win +9 pts; dist100 +0.42 t 2.2) and vanished in test (-0.09 to +0.05%, t within +-1), while the win-rate edge shrinks but stays positive (+2 to +6 pts). 1h and daily EMA features, Bollinger %B (+0.23% t 1.2 | -0.08%) and bandwidth: nothing. Luck expects ~0.2 SOLID and 1-2 WEAK. No book test run (no SOLID). EMA/Bollinger position is a hit-rate descriptor, not a mean-return signal.

## Report 371 (Nov 22 2026): option-buyer P&L on flips + gamma/IV filters (xs/report_371_option_buyer.py)
Research: Baltussen et al (intraday momentum strong on negative net-gamma days, insignificant on positive), Barbon-Buraschi Gamma Fragility (negative gamma = momentum, positive = reversal, stronger in illiquid names), volatility-risk-premium literature (long options profit ~28% of the time; IV > RV ~85% of days). Measure: real EOD quotes, buy the ~ATM call (14-45 DTE, nearest 21) at the ask on the flip-day close, exit at the bid at the vector exit or +5 trading days. 3,210 flips with a priced call (names with ThetaData quotes AND a GEX file): mean call P&L -4.1% (train -1.2% | test -5.0%), median -16%, profitable 31%, ~6.5% average bid-ask spread, while the underlying averaged +0.79% at the same flips. G1 negative net GEX -> better: opposite sign both halves (diff -1.0, t -0.2 | -6.7, t -2.1: positive-gamma flips paid calls better); V1 low IV/RV +3.1 (t 0.6) | -2.9 (t -0.9); V2 low IV +2.1 (t 0.4) | +2.1 (t 0.7). Combined filter (negative gamma AND cheap IV/RV): worse than all (-3.6% vs -1.2%; -7.2% vs -4.8%). 0 of 3 pass. An at-the-money 3-week call loses 16% at the median and wins about as often as the underlying flip does (31%); entry-bar filters tested so far do not rescue it.

## 372 ORB and noise-band intraday strategies (Nov 22 2026)
Declared: Zarattini-style ORB (stocks in play, bar0 colour, stop at bar0 extreme, exit 15:45) and Concretum noise band (14d noise, VWAP filter) on the 54 names, 15m, 4bp round trip. Pass = mean>0 and sleeve Sharpe>1 both halves, corr(vector)<0.3.
Result: FAIL. ORB long-only +0.3bp train / +4.6bp test per trade, sleeve Sharpe 0.25 / 0.46, DD -11%, corr +0.15; 8bp costs erase it. Long+short negative in train. Noise band loses in both halves (-3 / -5bp, win 28%), even gross it is ~2bp. 50/50 blends with the vector are below vector alone (1.84, 1.33 vs 2.60).
Why: published results rest on small/mid-cap, high-vol names and cheap-cost assumptions; on liquid mega-caps opening range is already efficiently priced. Low correlation to vector is real but there is no edge to diversify with.

## 373 Post-flip confirmation indicator (Nov 22 2026)
Rey's Pine (wait 2-10 bars, 2 closes > flip high+0.05ATR, progress>=0.5ATR, efficiency>=0.55, adverse<=0.5ATR). 29,288 bull flips (all 54 names, unfiltered by b302 gates), 4bp.
Only 26% confirm, median lag 2 bars. Confirm-entry mean +0.30% (win 36%) vs all flips at flip entry +0.22% (win 29%): train +0.34 vs +0.30, test +0.27 vs +0.16. The 'confirmed vs unconfirmed' gap at the ORIGINAL entry (+1.1 / +1.35 pts, t 10-13) is look-ahead (confirmation is defined by the future path) and not admissible. Tradable comparison is only the small per-trade gain with 74% fewer trades; needs a slot-book test.

## 374 Vector Quant Research Suite confirmations (Nov 22 2026)
DC leg, CUSUM, semivariance, change-point proxy, noise-RSI, and count>=3, evaluated at the flip bar on the 12,360 live flips (no look-ahead), aligned vs not.
Result: no admission. Only CUSUM has same-sign diffs (train +0.36pts t 2.2, test +0.01 t 0.1): the declared rule technically passes but the test effect is zero, so it is NOT admitted. DC, semivariance, change-point, RSI, count: signs flip between halves, |t|<1.5 mostly. Flags are mostly always-on (semi 74%, RSI 74%, CUSUM 63%), so they carry little information.

## 375 Outside list of 'room to run' conditions on the 12,360 large-cap flips (Nov 22 2026)
Volatility transition (VT), its change, first-expansion flag, variance ratio 4/16, efficiency shock, tail imbalance, volatility memory; two-sided terciles on train, week-clustered t.
Result: 0 SOLID, 0 WEAK by the declared rule. VT flips sign across halves (train +0.59pp t +2.8 | test -0.20pp t -1.8): variance expansion helped in 2019-21 and hurts in 2022+, a regime reversal, not a filter. Others |t|<=1.6 with mixed signs. Already covered earlier: survival (96/98), variance ratio (138/179), change point and semivariance (374).

## 376 Lead-Lag Propagation Index (LLPI), multi-symbol OHLC (Nov 22 2026)
Market residual (rolling beta vs SPY), sector residual (peers among the 54), delayed transmission theta (500-bar rolling lag-1 slope), PROP = theta x last-3-bar sector shock; plus THETA, SECRUN, STKRES, MKTRUN. 11,774 flips (SPY 15m ends Aug 15 2026), outcomes raw and beta-hedged, two-sided terciles on train.
Result: 0 SOLID, 0 WEAK of 10. All |t|<=1.7, signs mixed between halves. The transmission itself is absent at 15m: mean theta 0.007, positive in 52% of names-bars in both halves (coin flip), so there is no delayed sector-to-stock propagation to condition on. Stock-specific run-up (STKRES) leans negative in both halves for the hedged residual (t -1.1 | -1.7): residual-dominated run-ups are slightly worse, not better; untested beyond that.

## 377 Cross-Market Propagation Index (CMPI) (Nov 22 2026)
Outside proposal: stock return predicted from lagged SPY and lagged sector residual (rolling univariate slopes), normalised, atan-bounded; sector ETFs not in cache so sector = peers among the 54. MKTC, SECC, CMPI, acceleration, CMPI>50, raw and beta-hedged, 11,774 flips.
Result: 0 SOLID, 0 WEAK of 10 (all |t|<=1.9, mixed signs). Combined CMPI does not appear where components do not (CMPI diff +0.02 / +0.05, t 0.1 / 0.5). Predicted contributions average 0.075-0.078 sd per bar: the lagged cross-market information is nearly nil at 15m, same finding as LLPI (376).

## 378 Regime atlas v2 (Nov 22 2026)
Five regimes of report 32, outlets side by side (xs/report_378_regime_atlas2.py, tables in scratchpad atlas378.csv). Data fact: the SPY 15m cache has two bad days (May 16 and 18 2026, +-0.56 log, a scale break); excluded from the market stats.
Large-cap vector, raw %/trade R1..R5: 0.39 | 0.48 | 0.10 | 0.22 | 0.29 (hedged 0.14 | 0.16 | 0.08 | 0.06 | 0.14); win% 33 | 33 | 29 | 30 | 32. Weakest in R3 (tightening/bear, SPY -14.9%, 54% down days). NQ legs pts/trade (x unit): all legs 0.81 | 1.70 | 1.92 | 1.72 | 3.95; RA 3.1 | 14.6 | 13.2 | 9.4 | 13.5; V15a 0.1 | 3.1 | 5.8 | 5.4 | 7.9; S1 -1.1 | 2.7 | 5.3 | 6.2 | 19.1; M1 2.2 | 1.9 | 3.5 | 0.7 | 4.4.
Reading: the two families move in OPPOSITE directions. The long-only equity vector pays with the market (R2 boom best, R3 bear worst, rho +0.7 with SPY return). NQ legs are weakest in R1 (calm, VR5 1.04) and strongest in R5 / R3 (high range, mean-reverting-at-5d VR5 0.76-0.92): RA rho -1.0 with VR5. n=5 regimes, descriptive only: a hypothesis (equity vector = beta/market-up regime; NQ legs = range-expansion regime), not a result. R1 for NQ also has the fewest trades, so part of its weakness is thin data.

## 379 Equity vector: beta vs residual, supply, book beta (Nov 22 2026; xs/report_379_equity_beta.py)
(1) Per-flip beta-hedged edge by year: +0.06 | +0.34 | +0.07 | -0.04 | +0.22 | +0.03 | +0.17 | +0.06 (2019..2026); week-clustered t >= 2 only in 2020 (2.4), 2023 (2.3), 2025 (2.3); 2022 negative. Not "stable": positive in 7 of 8 years but mostly small and individually insignificant. Mean flip beta 1.2-1.4.
(2) Market part of raw per-flip mean: 63 | 68 | 38 | 74 | 58 % by regime R1..R5. Monthly corr(mean flip ret, SPY) +0.72, corr(residual, SPY) +0.32.
(3) Supply: flips/month 46 | 97 | 115 | 183 | 182 (b302). Flips per flipping name-month is flat 4.3-5.2; distinct names with flips rise 16 | 25 | 30 | 46 | 49. 43 of 54 names have first flip after mid-2019. The rise is universe composition (names entering the data / eligibility), NOT a change in signal frequency per name. Cause of late entry not verified (likely data start or the options-ratio gate).
(4) BOOK level (6 slots, daily): beta to SPY only 0.14 (rolling 126d 0.03-0.33, ~0.2 since 2021), corr +0.33. Book Sharpe 2.64 vs beta-hedged 2.53, hedged return 20.2%/yr. By regime book ann / hedged ann: R1 30.7/30.3 (Sharpe 7.4, ~10 names/month, in-sample era), R2 33.6/30.0, R3 3.3/6.5 (SPY -12%), R4 20.0/15.7, R5 25.7/20.9.
(5) Conditional on SPY state at the flip (raw | hedged): above 200d +0.27|+0.10, below +0.44|+0.14; SPY vol low/mid/high +0.19|+0.08, +0.26|+0.10, +0.46|+0.14: more expectancy in stress, not in calm up-markets.
Reading: the live book is already close to market-neutral (beta ~0.15-0.2) because slots are limited and often partly in cash, so market exposure does not need managing; the regime swings in the book are mostly real (R3 weak), and the early 'train' era was a ~16-name universe.

## 380 Fixed-universe backtest of the live 6-slot book (Nov 22 2026; xs/report_380_fixed_universe.py)
Why 43 of 54 names have no early flips (it is the gate, not missing data): optvol ratio rows exist from 2018-01-02 for most, but options volume/shares was below 1.5 in the early years (NFLX median 0.25 in 2019H1, AMD 0.96, JPM 0.84, UNH 0.78); 8 names are late listings (ABNB, APP, COIN, CRWD, GEV, HOOD, PLTR, UBER); AVGO, QCOM, PYPL have no ratio rows in the 2018 files. Flips per flipping name-month stays ~5; the 25 -> 190 monthly growth is names becoming eligible.
Same rules, four universes (CAGR / DD / Sharpe; 2019-21 | 2022-26): A all 54 live 23.6% / -23.5% / 2.60 (33.8 | 17.5, Sh 4.15 | 1.85); B 46 early-listed 21.6% / -22.7% / 2.44 (33.8 | 14.4); C CORE 14 names eligible by 2019 21.7% / -15.7% / 2.78 (34.8 | 14.2, Sh 4.27 | 1.87), slots 91% filled; D LATE 40 entrants 7.5% / -20.2% / 1.04 (15.0 | 3.0), slots 70% filled. Beta to SPY 0.08-0.14 in all. By regime CORE: R1 +24 | R2 +42 | R3 +18 | R4 +18 | R5 +6 CAGR; A: +25 | +39 | +3 | +22 | +26.
Per-name: top 5 names = 46% of the live book's contribution (NVDA 34, CMG 20, AAPL 19, FDX 16, BA 15 in sum-of-trades/6 pct points); core names 66%, late names 34%.
Reading: (1) the ~2.6 Sharpe does NOT come from the gradual arrival of better names: the fixed 14-name core gives 2.78 and the same train-to-test decay (4.3 -> 1.9), so the decay is not composition either. (2) Late entrants are weaker on their own (Sharpe 1.04); adding them raises exposure (cash 9% -> 6%) and the union beats either piece in R5 (A +26% vs CORE +6% / LATE -2%), i.e. more eligible names mostly help by filling slots. (3) Dropping the 8 recent listings costs ~3 pts of test CAGR. (4) The core is today's winners (NVDA, AAPL, AMZN, GOOGL, CMG): survivorship in the name list is a bigger unresolved bias than the composition effect; no point-in-time index history exists in the workspace to remove it.

## 381 Is the point-in-time options-ratio gate a survivorship filter? (Nov 23 2026; xs/report_381_gate_vs_pool.py)
Rey's point: the PIT gate removes survivorship, and the first PIT-universe test was good. Check inside the same 168-name wide_T data pool (all names have data today, so pool survivorship is common to every group). 6 slots, 8% breaker, 2+2bp, ratio-rank order (no brank): GATED (S&P at the time and ratio>=1.5; 54 names) 30.3% CAGR / -17.8% / Sharpe 3.05 (2019-21 46.3% | 2022-26 21.2%); BELOW GATE (S&P, ratio 1.0-1.5; 80 names) -1.6% / -47.1% / -0.16 (12.5% | -9.5%); NOT S&P at the time, ratio>=1.5 (60 names) 10.5% / -51.4% / 0.77 (58.3% | -12.0%); all S&P PIT flips ratio ignored (95 names) 23.8% / -24.2% / 2.49 (40.7% | 14.2%).
Reading: Rey is right. The same pool, same sim and same data-survivor property give nothing for S&P names under the gate (2022-26 -9.5%), so the returns are not pool survivorship; the gate (information known at the flip) is doing the selecting, and the PIT S&P flag matters too (non-S&P high-ratio names fail in 2022-26). Residual survivorship that this cannot test: delisted/acquired S&P members absent from the pool, and the ratio gate being correlated with past attention/winning. The absolute figures here (30.3%) differ from the live book (23.6%) because the live book's extra entry rules are not reproduced; only the group comparison is the point.

## 382 2x2 factorial (S&P at the flip x options ratio >= 1.5) and the 17,391 vs 12,360 reconciliation (Nov 23 2026; xs/report_382_factorial.py)
Reconciliation: b302 has no row that is not in wide_T; 4,362 of 17,447 wide_T gated rows are absent from b302 and ALL of them are 1H-vector flips (h1 True). The live 15m book = the 15m-type rows with both filters: 12,326 rows, 23.7% / -23.5% / Sharpe 2.61 (reproduces b302's 23.6 / 2.60). b301/b302 also drop rows missing the seven PIT signals. wide_T only holds ratio >= 1.0, so "below gate" = 1.0-1.5.
Trade level, 15m rows, mean flip return % (train | test): S&P ratio 1.0-1.5 +0.16 | +0.04; S&P ratio>=1.5 +0.46 | +0.20; not-S&P 1.0-1.5 +0.27 | +0.24; not-S&P >=1.5 +0.52 | +0.12. OLS (pp, week-clustered): train SP -0.11 (t -0.6), GATE +0.25 (t 1.2), SPxGATE +0.05 (0.2); test SP -0.19 (t -1.8), GATE -0.11 (t -1.1), SPxGATE +0.27 (t +2.4). So in 2019-21 the gate helps both groups and S&P adds nothing; the interaction (gate helps ONLY inside the S&P) is a 2022-26 effect.
Portfolio, common pool, 15m rows (CAGR / DD / Sharpe | 2019-21 | 2022-26): everything 9.0 / -54.1 / 0.78 | 56.5 | -13.3; S&P only 18.0 / -23.9 / 1.93 | 33.4 | 9.2; gate only 20.6 / -44.9 / 1.52 | 67.8 | -2.2; both 23.7 / -23.5 / 2.61 | 33.8 | 17.7. In train the gate alone has the best return (67.8%, Sharpe 4.65) and both-filters is lower (Sharpe 4.15); the combined filter wins only after 2022 and on drawdown (-23.5 vs -44.9). Caution: the combined filter's advantage is visible only in the test half.

## 383 Merging the 1H flips into the live book (Nov 23 2026; xs/report_383_merge_1h.py)
Pool = S&P at flip and ratio >= 1.5, 6 slots, 8% breaker. A 15m only (live backtest) 23.7% / -23.5% / 2.61; B all rows with no one-slot rule 31.8% / -15.7% / 3.14; C all rows, 15m first, ONE position per name (the live rule) 23.7% / -21.4% / 2.56 (2019-21 37.8, 2022-26 15.5); D 1H only one per name 16.7% / -23.8% / 2.28. Daily-return correlation A vs C 0.91.
Reading: the apparent +8 pts CAGR from adding 1H flips (B) comes from letting the same name hold two slots (a 15m and a 1H position, i.e. doubling up on the same winners), not from new opportunities; under the live one-slot-per-name rule the 1H flips add nothing. First attempt had a duplicate-index bug (rows C/D inflated); fixed before reporting.

## Report 384 (Nov 23 2026): concentration stress on the live vector book
Hypothesis: edge survives losing its best names. Base 23.6% CAGR / Sharpe 2.60 (train 4.15, test 1.85). Minus NVDA/CMG/AAPL/FDX/BA: CAGR 14.4%, Sharpe 1.75 (test 1.12) - passes the pre-set kill (Sharpe >= 1.0) but test half is thin. Random 5-name removal x60: Sharpe median 2.49 [2.06-2.74], so the top five matter beyond chance. Leave-one-out: Sharpe 2.18 (GS) to 2.80 (ADBE), no single-name dependence; FDX, a "top contributor" by flip return, lowers book Sharpe when removed-in-LOO (slot competition). Verdict: broad across names, but the top-5 cluster carries ~40% of CAGR and most of the test-half margin.

## Report 385 (Nov 23 2026): drop the top-5 from index-like baskets
Rey: top names also lead SPY/QQQ, so is the concentration just "biggest movers"? Dropping NVDA/CMG/AAPL/FDX/BA from a plain hold of the 54 names loses ~0% CAGR (equal-weight 28.0% -> 28.0%; dollar-volume-weighted cap proxy 23.3% -> 23.4%), train and test alike. The same five cost the vector book 39% of CAGR (23.6 -> 14.4), so the dependence is vector-specific (entry timing on those names), not a buy-and-hold drift effect. Caveat: true SPY/QQQ constituent weights are not in the workspace (proxy only), and the 54-name hold (28% CAGR, Sharpe 1.11) beats SPY (15.6%, 0.87) and QQQ (22.4%, 0.97) - the research set itself is a strong, today's-liquid-names list, so survivorship/selection bias in the set remains the open exposure.

Part A: taken flips +0.32% mean vs skipped +0.25% (gap +0.06pp; train -0.01pp, test +0.03pp); on days with both, taken beats the skipped mean on 57% (train) / 52% (test) of days; share of >+3% flips is 12.4% taken vs 12.2% skipped. Needs volume or market-cap history for the full pool.

## Report 387 (Nov 23 2026): follow-through persistence in the vector's own track record
Rey: confirmations built on volume, volatility, macro, options are arbitrary models of follow-through. Hypothesis: follow-through is persistent in the vector's own outcomes (clean ratchet resolution in trending tape, repeated failure in chop). Features strictly past (exit < entry): same-name last-3 mean return, last-flip win, cross-section last-30 mean return and win rate. b302, terciles on train, week-clustered t. Result: 0 of 4 admitted; every |t| <= 1.4; train diffs all negative (-0.14 to -0.43pp), test diffs mixed (+0.16, +0.07, -0.09, -0.04). No persistence either way.

## Report 388 (Nov 23 2026): pivot geometry + higher-timeframe vector state
Rey's hypothesis: a 15m bull flip while the higher-TF vector is still bearish (market dumped, HTF not flipped) follows through better; the vector's own geometry carries follow-through. 12,360 b302 flips matched. HTF: 1H (bull vs not) diff -0.05pp train / -0.02 test (t -0.3/-0.2); 4H bull vs bear +(-0.10)/+0.10 (t -0.5/+1.0); daily +0.06/+0.09 (t 0.3/0.8); any HTF not flipped vs all bullish -0.01/-0.08 (t -0.1/-0.7). 60% of flips have the 4H vector bearish, 48% the daily bearish, so 'not flipped' is the majority state, not a rare dump signal. Geometry (top vs bottom third): room in ATR +0.24/-0.05pp; break strength G2 +0.49pp (t 2.2) train but -0.16pp (t -1.5) test: sign flip, not admitted; prior-bear ratchet count -0.02/+0.07; prior bear-run length +0.32/+0.15 (t 1.6/1.3), same sign, below threshold. 0 of 8 admitted. Lower timeframe NOT tested: no 5m cache for the names (QQQ only).

Rey: model market shape. Replaced the fixed 8-bar pivots with causal 0-dim persistence (zig-zag filtration) pivots confirmed at k x ATR retracement, k in {1,2,3} (pre-registered), everything else identical (exits, gate, 54 names, 6 slots, 8% breaker). ## Report 392 (Nov 23 2026): rejection vs break at the flip
Rey: decide if a flip will be a rejection or a break. Label from the vector's own level: REJ = a 15m close back below the broken bear level within the first 4 entry bars (1h). 12,360 flips: rejection rate 38.3% train / 39.6% test; mean flip return REJ -0.51%/-0.56% vs BRK +1.06%/+0.73% (the label is the whole story of the flip return). Pre-entry features, top vs bottom third, REJ rate and return diff (train | test): prior tests of the level (bear run) 38/37% rejection, ret diff 0.00pp (t 0.0) | 39/41%, +0.05 (0.4); prior tests last 30 bars +0.15 (1.0) | +0.04 (0.4); flip-bar close location -0.06 (-0.3) | -0.05 (-0.5); flip-bar body/ATR REJ 33 vs 42% train, 37 vs 43% test (same sign, ~6-9pp lower rejection for big bodies) but return diff +0.19 (1.0) | -0.16 (-1.4); upper-wick fraction +0.06 (0.3) | +0.05 (0.5). 0 of 5 admitted. The bar anatomy of the break is the only pre-entry quantity with a consistent (small) effect on the rejection rate, and it does not carry through to return.

Range = last confirmed swing low/high (A: causal zig-zag at 2 ATR, B: vector's own 8-bar pivots), width >= 2 ATR; sweep = low below L0 and a close back above within 2 bars; flip within 20 or 40 bars after. 12,360 flips. ## Report 396 (Nov 23 2026): consolidation (Wyckoff/LuxAlgo definition) and the AMD sequence on 15m/1H/4H/daily
Definition fixed before the run from published procedures (luxalgo trading-range and AMD pages): 20 TF bars, width <= 4 ATR, >= 2 separated touches in each edge zone, Kaufman ER <= 0.30; CONS = valid range in the last 5 TF bars; SEQ = within 10 TF bars a low-side sweep with a close back above the range low and then a close above the range midpoint, before the flip. Shares of flips: CONS 34% (15m) / 24% (1h) / 24% (4h) / 26% (D), any 72%; SEQ 7.1% / 1.8% / 1.1% / 1.6%, any 11.3%. Yes-minus-no flip return train | test (t): CONS_15m +0.29 (1.7) | -0.08 (-0.9); CONS_1h +0.15 (0.8) | -0.14 (-1.6); CONS_4h -0.08 | +0.02; CONS_D -0.12 | -0.13 (-1.4); CONS_any +0.14 | -0.12; 2+ TFs consolidating vs none +0.19 (0.9) | -0.19 (-1.9). SEQ_15m -0.14 | -0.02; SEQ_1h +0.41 (0.8, n 59) | +0.36 (1.0, n 161); SEQ_4h +1.15 (1.6, n 34) | +0.46 (1.0, n 107); SEQ_D -0.57 | +0.27; SEQ_any +0.11 (0.5) | +0.13 (1.0). 0 of 12 admitted. Post-hoc pooled lead (chosen after seeing the table, so NOT admitted): HTF sequence on 1h/4h/D = 4.4% of flips (121 train | 426 test): +0.84% vs +0.44% (diff +0.40pp, t 1.2) | +0.55% vs +0.21% (+0.35pp, t 1.7), win rate 36 vs 33% | 34 vs 31%: same sign in both halves, small n. Needs an out-of-sample check (2018 flips, mid/small-cap book, forward log).

## Report 398 (Nov 23 2026): CNN-BiLSTM on the flip windows (pre-registered)
31,105 large-cap flips (54 names, ungated), 64 x 15m bars before each flip, 7 scale-free channels (OHLC vs flip close / ATR, volume vs median, vector regime, vector level distance). Train 2019-21 only (9,658 flips, last 15% by time = early-stop validation, val IC +0.008/+0.011/+0.037 across 3 seeds), test 2018 (2,957) and 2022-26 (18,490) never seen. Shuffled-label control (2 seeds, val IC -0.029/-0.018). Test Spearman IC: 2018 -0.017 (weekly t -0.3), 2022-26 -0.001 (weekly t -0.8); control -0.043 / -0.015. Top-minus-bottom tercile diff: 2018 -0.16pp (t -0.9), 2022-26 +0.20pp (t +2.0, but IC ~0 and control +0.08); gated (ratio>=1.5): 2018 +0.99pp (t 1.7, n 341; control +1.04pp t 2.2), 2022-26 +0.17pp (t 1.4; control +0.05). AUC 0.489 / 0.518 (control 0.501 / 0.526). Declared pass not met: no positive IC with |t|>=2 in either test period; the only tercile result at t 2.0 (2022-26) is one period of two and rank-uncorrelated with return. In-sample the model scores IC +0.031 / diff +0.33pp (t 2.9) and loses it out of sample. Verdict: raw price windows carry nothing beyond what is already in the vector's state.

## Report 399 (Nov 23 2026): pooled cross-market CNN / CNN-BiLSTM / GBM on flips (pre-registered)
Pooled large caps (A) + sub-$5 small caps (B): 78,429 windows (the first build lost 40% of flips to NaN SPY pre-market bars - fixed by forward-filling SPY - and the numbers below are from the full build), 9 channels incl. SPY co-movement and rolling 20-bar correlation with SPY, scalars: consolidation/AMD flags, book flag, log price, options ratio (A), count of pooled flips in the prior 60 min (clustering). Train 2019-21 (A 9,658 + B 17,282), last 15% by time = validation, tests A 2018, A 2022-26 (+gated), B 2022-26 (+px<$5 book), never touched. Validation IC (adjacent 2021H2 block): CNN +0.057/+0.052/+0.060, BiLSTM +0.064/+0.081/+0.090, GBM +0.067; controls +0.012 to +0.018. Test IC (model: M1 CNN / M2 CNN-BiLSTM / M3 GBM): A 2018 -0.054 / +0.057 / -0.085; A 2022-26 -0.053 (weekly t -3.1) / -0.028 (-3.4) / -0.025; B book 2022-26 +0.008 / +0.012 / +0.004. Top-minus-bottom tercile diff (t): A 2022-26 +0.10 (0.9) / -0.24 (-2.3) / +0.17 (1.8); gated A +0.04 / -0.15 / +0.19 (1.5); B book +0.11 (0.9) / -0.16 (-1.2) / +0.09 (0.8); the shuffled controls land in the same +-0.2pp band (A 2018 control M1 +0.43pp t +2.5 shows how noisy that period is). AUC 0.47-0.52 everywhere. Declared pass (IC>0 and diff>0 with t>=2 in both A 2022-26 and B book, not negative in A 2018) not met by any model: validation IC of +0.06-0.09 collapses to ~0 or negative out of time (non-stationarity between the validation block and 2022-26). Cross-market channels, clustering counts and consolidation flags did not rescue it; with report 398 this closes the price-only neural-net route on the flips.

## Report 400: small-cap (sub-$5) slot book with consolidation gate/priority (xs/report_400_smallcap_cons_book.py)
8 slots, equal weight compounding, 40bp round trip, ungated sub-$5 flips on 108 LSE names, split 2019-21 | 2022-26 (no 2018 data). BASE CAGR -46% / -35%. Consolidation gate (any TF) -45% / -26%; C_15m&C_1h gate +9% / -12%; priority -44% / -28%. Per-trade net: cons -15bp (t -1.7) / -17bp (t -3.2) vs no-cons -47bp / -42bp. VERDICT: consolidation is a relative improvement in both halves but no absolute edge after costs; the ungated flips are not the live screener, so this does not clear the book. Lead not admitted.

## Report 401: attribution stress test, vector vs universe vs beta (xs/report_401_build.py, report_401_attrib.py)
True daily mark-to-market of the live 6-slot book (entry px -> daily closes -> exit px, 2bp/side, to 2026-08-14): CAGR 27.6%, Sharpe 1.36, MDD -25.5% (the earlier smoothed-spread series gave Sharpe 2.6; that was an artefact of spreading each trade evenly over its hold days, so ALL earlier book Sharpes/betas on spread() are overstated; CAGR comparable).
Exposure-matched mean-return steps per year: SPY x w 22.5% (of which +5.7% is the book's pro-cyclical exposure: w=0.51 in Covid, 0.83 in 2022 bear) | universe (54 names) +14.3% | S&P+ratio gate -5.9% | vector book vs gate -4.6% | book 26.3%. Beta to SPY 0.62.
Random-timing control (same names, same hold lengths, entry shifted +-120d): actual CAGR rank 63% (73% if gate-eligible required), MDD better than control in 75-84% of draws: vector timing is directionally helpful, not significant. 
Universe check: EW S&P members of the 54 names 22.9% vs the other 108 pool names 17.6% vs SPY 16.0% (2022-26: 16.3 / 14.9 / 11.3): the 54-name set carries a real selection premium (hindsight) that fades post-2022.
Rey proposal (ratio picks, vector classifies): ratio-top6 always 29.5% CAGR, MDD -39%; + 15m vector bull-only filter 11.5%, MDD -36%: the 15m regime is not a usable risk filter; the flip-entry book (-25.5% MDD, Covid -9%, 2022 -13%) is what protects. Breaker off: MDD -23.3%, Sharpe 1.26, so the breaker adds nothing to drawdown; the protection is the vector exits plus not re-entering.

## Reports 402-405 (Nov 23 2026): five paper-based ideas on the live 6-slot book (true daily MTM; train 2019-21 | test 2022-26 to Aug 14 2026; live: CAGR 27.6%, Sharpe 1.36, MDD -25.5%, win 30.7%, median trade -0.62% net, top-5 names 47% of P&L, CAGR ex-top5 18.0%)
Literature (abstracts): Moreira-Muir vol-managed portfolios; Gilchrist-Zakrajsek / Wellington credit-vol harmony; Xiong 2026 (arXiv 2605.20636, 10y-yield change + SPY drawdown + VIX stress relief timing); Bollerslev-Tauchen-Zhou VRP; Li-Wang 2026 (arXiv 2608.26115, IV spread/skew); correlation/HRP diversification.
402 (declared thresholds from train): I1a/b vol-managed exposure: MDD -24.5/-20.0, CAGR 23.4/22.5, Calmar worse in 19-21: fail. I2 credit (HYG/IEF) vector bull gate: CAGR 6.9%, top5 94%: fail. I3 low avg corr gate: CAGR 9.2%: fail. I4a high VRP: MDD -15.3 but CAGR 13.6: fail. I4b VIX stress relief (VIX <= 20d mean): CAGR 24.5%, Sharpe 1.51, MDD -13.2, worst-12m -4.2% vs -17.5%, half MDD -9.7|-13.2 vs -11.3|-25.5, Calmar 2.36|1.94 vs 3.29|0.86: near-miss (fails half-1 Calmar only). I5 corr-aware slots (skip max corr > .75): CAGR 29.1%, MDD -23.3, Calmar up both halves, MDD down in 22-26 only, top5 unchanged.
403 VIX relief: relief10d/5d-ago neighbours also cut MDD both halves, VIX LEVEL < 20 does not (it is the change, not the level); 100 random block gates: real MDD better than 98% of null, Calmar 98%; relief20 + I5a: MDD -13.2, worst-12m -1.5%.
404 NAME-LEVEL vol relief (own asset; vol models from earlier reports): N1 close-RV10 <= 20d mean MDD -21.0 CAGR 27.4; N2 15m-RV 5d <= 20d mean CAGR 29.4% Sharpe 1.66 MDD -15.6% worst-12m +1.1%, half MDD -8.4|-15.6, CAGR halves 39.2|23.4 (live 37.1|21.9); N3 HAR forecast <= 22d mean (pooled coefs fit on train) CAGR 29.4% Sharpe 1.55 MDD -14.2%, halves 38.7|23.8. N1, N2, N3 all PASS the declared rule (Calmar up and MDD down in both halves, win rate/median not lower, top5 share 44-51%). SPY RV10 relief (market, no VIX) fails; stacking VIX relief AND name relief over-filters (CAGR 13.9-19.3%): fail. Null (60 random per-name block gates, same keep): N2 MDD better than 95%, Calmar 100%, Sharpe 97%.
405 N2 neighbours: windows (3,20), (5,10), (5,40), (10,20): MDD lower than live in BOTH halves in all four, but CAGR falls in 2022-26 for three (15.0-15.5% vs 21.9%) so only (10,20) passes the full rule: 1/4. READ: drawdown reduction from name-level vol relief is robust across windows and three vol models; return preservation is NOT robust to the window (5d/20d is the best case, partly a lucky corner). Trade-level win rate (30-32%) and median (-0.6%) did not move under any idea; the book is a payoff-skew system, the improvement is in path (DD, worst-12m), not hit rate.

## Report 415 (Nov 24 2026) - signal-based exit: daily Hawkes intensity death + vector exit (no time holds, per Rey)
Declared: event = ret >= 1 sigma20 and volume >= 20d mean; lambda decays tau=5d; exit when lambda <= 0.25 x peak since entry (peak >= 1) or the live vector exit, whichever first. Neighbours tau 3/8, theta .15/.40.
Result: live avg hold 2.97d, so a daily Hawkes rarely fires (66-557 of 12,320 trades). Trade level win 30.6-31.1% vs 30.6%, median -0.63% unchanged, every era identical. Book: base CAGR 27.6% Sh 1.36 MDD -25.5%; MIN(5,.25) 24.9% / 1.26 / -23.2%; (3,.25) 24.2 / 1.25 / -28.5; (8,.25) 26.8 / 1.32 / -25.2; (5,.15) 27.6 / 1.36 / -24.7; (5,.40) 24.8 / 1.28 / -27.8. 2022-26 half CAGR lower in 4/5. FAIL: no gain, daily timescale mismatched to ~3-day holds. Untested: 15m Hawkes (intraday excitation) as the exit.

## Reports 416-417 (Nov 24 2026) - overextension both sides; Hawkes exit on 15m bars (signal exits only, Rey: no time holds)
416 (declared EXT = 20d ret >= 2 sigma_20d and 5d vol >= 1.5x 60d): only 0.5% of trades (79 long / 68 short) -> untestable. Vector bear-flip SHORTS on the 54 names lose in general (win 26.2%, mean -0.10%, selection bias: gate winners). 416b looser second look (1.0 sigma, 1.2x vol, exploratory): EXT share 3.4%; LONG EXT-nonEXT +0.16pp (t +0.6); SHORT EXT-nonEXT +0.63pp (t +1.4), 2022 era 16.7% win. FAIL / not significant.
417 (15m Hawkes death exit, tau 8 bars, theta .25, MIN with vector exit): win 30.6 -> 42.8%, median -0.63 -> -0.31%, win up in every era (43.1/45.5/40.8/42.3) BUT hold 2.97d -> 0.52d, mean 0.24 -> 0.03%, book CAGR 27.6 -> 8.4%, Sh 1.36 -> 0.69 (neighbours CAGR 4-11%, MDD -17 to -30%). 417b second look, Hawkes death only when underwater: win 28.1%, med -0.49%, CAGR 21.2%, Sh 1.16, MDD -22.3% (tau8 th.25); neighbours CAGR 16-17%. Lower win rate than live-plus-hawkes, still below live CAGR; worst 12m -11.7% vs -17.5%. No pass.
Lesson: win rate and median can be raised (Hawkes exit) only by sacrificing mean; the edge is right-tail.

## Report 421 (Nov 24 2026) - options-ratio gate grid: window N in {1,5,20,60,252} x theta in {1,1.5,2,3,4}, train-only selection
Universe: wide_T 15m PIT-S&P flips, 21,546 flips. Swing family (vector exit, all flips): train mean net per trade by gate ~0.26-0.34% for N<=60 at any theta<=2, rising to 0.40-0.45% at theta 3-4; N=252 theta 1.5 = 0.42% (n2768), theta 4 = 0.48% (n562). Train pick = (252, 4): test win 30.5 / med -0.70 / mean 0.20% (n1824) vs live (252,1.5) 30.1 / -0.66 / 0.16% (n9363): +0.088pp, t +0.9 -> not adopted (fewer trades, thinner). Window length adds nothing at theta>=2; the 252d median at 1.5 is the only config clearly above shorter windows in train. Decision: keep live gate (252, 1.5).
SD family (same-session vector exit) is INVALID: conditioning on the exit being same-day selects stop-outs (win 1-2%), an outcome-conditioned partition, so nothing about an intraday ratio can be read from it. A proper intraday ratio test needs 1m/5m flips with their own intraday exits (pull running; lse_5m / lse_1m in the scratchpad).

Proxy: volume-weighted call-minus-put gamma_net (greeks_daily), own 252d PIT percentile; NEG-proxy pct <= .33, positive-gamma-proxy pct >= .5; 15m, ratio-gated, one trade per episode, 24 names (incl QQQ), entries to 2026-10-14. VALIDATION 2018-2025 (G20 X1): base n1318 win 32.2% med -0.59 mean +0.37 (real-gex model +0.31); vol relief N2dn n694 mean +0.61 vs expansion n624 +0.11 (same direction as the real-gamma result); G20 X3 base win 44.5% mean +0.25 (real 43.9 / +0.20). => proxy reproduces the 2018-25 pattern.
2026 YTD (to Oct 14): G20 X1 base n126 win 38.9% med -0.54 mean +0.62%; X3 n126 win 47.6% med -0.19 mean +0.30%; G252 X1 n211 win 34.1% mean +0.37; G252 X3 n211 win 42.7% mean +0.17. May-Oct: G20 X1 n49 win 42.9% mean +1.12; X3 n49 win 53.1% med +0.14 mean +0.78. VOL RELIEF REVERSED in 2026: G20 X1 relief n73 mean +0.11 vs expansion n53 +1.32; G20 X3 relief +0.24 vs expansion +0.39 (G252 X1 relief -0.02 vs +1.00). Consistent with the live-book N2 gate hurting 2026. n small, proxy not real gamma, no significance claim.

## Report 432 (Nov 24 2026) - WHEN does vol relief work? relief vs expansion by risk regime (SPY vs 200d), options-flow model (chain-proxy gamma, 15m, ratio-gated, one trade/episode)
Hypothesis declared: relief helps in RISK-OFF, expansion helps in RISK-ON. RESULT: relief - expansion in RISK-OFF = G20 X1 +1.29pp (t +2.7; relief n192 win 38.5% mean +1.25% vs expansion n175 win 29.1% mean -0.04%), G20 X3 +0.66 (t +2.1), G252 X1 +0.83 (t +2.5), G252 X3 +0.49 (t +2.0); same sign in train and test in all four (train +2.88/+0.59/+2.60/+0.97, test +1.07/+0.72/+0.59/+0.45). RISK-ON: ~0 (+0.03, -0.07, +0.06, -0.03; |t|<0.5) - expansion does NOT help in risk-on in 2018-25. 2026 (87% risk-on): relief minus expansion -1.2pp X1 (t -1.6), -0.0 X3: the 2026 reversal is a risk-on X1 effect not seen in 2018-25 risk-on; risk-off 2026 n=18. PASS the risk-off half, FAIL the risk-on half. Use: relief as a RISK-OFF classifier (take only relief flips when SPY < 200d), no filter in risk-on.
Rey: live screener since deploy has cut losses over both drops (site screenshot: Screener book -7.39% vs SPY -10.36%, QQQ -17.20% from Oct 13); he has a daily gamma source via his broker API.

## Reports 454/455 CORRECTION and rerun (2026-11-24)
The first run of 454/455 (entries above for 454) compared split-adjusted LSE prices with unadjusted theta zero-gamma levels (NVDA x40, TSLA x15, AAPL x4, AMZN x20 pre-split), so BELOW was nearly always true on those names. SUPERSEDED. Fixed by dividing theta spot/ZG by the split factor (spot / LSE daily close, rounded; factors seen TSLA 3/15, NVDA 10/40, AAPL 4, AMZN 20). Daily-sign baseline and reports 452/453 use only the sign of net gamma and were unaffected.
Corrected 454, 15m (4,412 trades): BASE daily neg vs pos +10.2bp t 2.67 (train t1.2, test t2.5) passes; HA below ZG vs above +7.3bp t 1.78 (NOT a pass, weaker than the daily sign); HB recent cross -8.7bp t -1.06; HC room top vs bottom +3.3bp t 0.51; HD vol +2.7bp t 0.38. 1h (1,118): BASE t 0.71, HA t 0.25, HB +12.1bp t 1.30 (train t 2.4, test t -0.4), HC room top vs bottom -22.4bp t -2.49 (wrong direction), HD t 0.43.
Report 455 (Rey: high vol + large room below ZG + recent cross -> goes further), corrected: 15m E1 (below & high IV & room top tercile, cutoff from 2019-21) n302 RETURN -9.8bp t -2.36 (train -2.5, test -13.5), MFE -13.5bp t -2.47; E2 (+recent cross) n34 RETURN -34bp t -3.06, MFE -2.4bp t -0.10. 1h E1 n76 return -4.4bp t -0.54, MFE -14.8bp t -2.27. The conjunction does the OPPOSITE of the hypothesis (underperforms, runs less far); the script's PASS tag only checks |t|>=2 with consistent sign, not the declared direction. Hypothesis rejected. Only the plain daily net-gamma sign survives (and only modestly: +10bp/trade on the underlying).
